The pin was fixed once at I1 and is not moved.

## 1. Consumability

Registry probes were made with `curl -sS -L` (status read with `-w '%{http_code}'`), HTTPS only, no credentials, on 2026-11-13. Source reads come from a partial clone of the pin (clone date 2026-11-12). Each registry has a control probe of something known to exist, so that a 404 means absence and not "listing unsupported".

The `.asc` presence was checked on five files (bom pom, outcome module, outcome-jvm jar, logging core-jvm jar, testing module); the other variants were listed, not each checked. These artifacts are transitive dependencies of the mail modules (Section 2), which is why they are reported.

### Q1.c Answer

**Is any mail module published as a Maven artifact: no.** None of the five registries lists a mail artifact. The mail modules apply only a local convention plugin with no publishing plugin: `thunderbird.library.jvm.gradle.kts` lines 4-10 apply `java-library`, the Kotlin JVM plugin and quality plugins [C-003]; the IMAP module applies `ThunderbirdPlugins.Library.jvm` [C-004]; at the pin the only hits for `maven-publish|signing|publishing` under `mail` and `build-plugin` are the APK-signing helper and one README link, and there are 0 hits for `maven-publish|publishing` in any `*.gradle.kts` or `*.toml` [C-005]. Consuming the code therefore means vendoring source, a composite build or a submodule, or self-publishing; that choice is analysed in Sections 2, 4 and 9.

### Q1.d Provenance: "Thunderbird bought the old K-9 app and its libraries"

**Verdict: partly correct.** Both 2022 announcements say K-9 Mail joined Thunderbird and that Thunderbird became its legal home, with the repository moved to the Thunderbird organisation on GitHub. None of the three announcement pages describes a purchase, a sale or a price: the words "acquired", "bought" and "purchase" do not occur in the k9mail.app post or the FAQ, and occur once on the announcement blog page inside a reader's comment about Mozilla, not in the post. So the transfer of ownership and stewardship is correct, and "bought" is not supported by the primary sources (absence of the word does not prove that no payment took place). Quoted primary-source sentences:

- "Thunderbird is now the legal home of K-9 Mail." [C-025]
- "The source code repository will be moved to the Thunderbird organization on GitHub." [C-025]
- "today, K-9 Mail officially joins the Thunderbird family." [C-026]

The FAQ is cited for context [C-027]. The pages are dated 2022-06-13.

**Continuation repository:** `thunderbird/thunderbird-android`. `https://api.github.com/repos/k9mail/k-9` answers 301 to repository id 1326671, whose `full_name` is `thunderbird/thunderbird-android`, not archived, default branch `main`, licence SPDX `Apache-2.0` [C-028]. The app's source fork `jberkel/k-9` is a GitHub fork (`fork: true`) [C-032].

### Q1.e Position of the vendored commit

The vendored commit `eaf689025ec28d55e3b62f5ea3b1f185f410ee6b` (branch `sms-backup-plus-1.5.11`, `<repo>/k9mail-vendored/NOTICE:5-6`, dated 2018-03-13) is **11 commits ahead of and 15032 commits behind** the pin, which is the `main` head. Merge base: `23bf474d9389fa658b7395cf2637c0ec88d45709` (2016-10-20, "Merge pull request #1735 from jberkel/use-properties-for-config"). Counts come from `git rev-list --left-right --count <pin>...eaf689025e` (local, authoritative) and agree with the GitHub compare API (`status: diverged`, `ahead_by: 11`, `behind_by: 15032`) [C-029]. The compare page (`https://github.com/thunderbird/thunderbird-android/compare/ac1f0e85f63c1b600ac443d1fb2d939bffff7de5...jberkel:k-9:eaf689025ec28d55e3b62f5ea3b1f185f410ee6b`) answered 200; its counts are drawn by script and are not in the static page, so the numbers rest on the API and the local count [C-030].

The 11 fork-only commits, oldest first: 737c582cc1 Add XOAuth2 [C-033]; ac837a0156 SDK 7 compat fixes; c16449248c Set internal date on APPEND [C-034]; 30985d7ff0 Just fetch the date header [C-035]; ccff8da295 Make classes public / protected [C-036]; 75bb293984 Publish to bintray; 605b321574 Fail early if username/password not set; 013948a758 Increae read timeout; 69eef8239b Retry socket connections; 7e6d627a7a No longer needed, publishing via jitpack.io now; eaf689025e Fix potential ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException. Three of them (internal date on APPEND, date-header fetch, public/protected visibility) are the behaviours Section 5 tests against current upstream. **Rework note (IR1, 2026-11-13):** the other seven were first set aside by subject line only; they were read in rework round 1 (the commit and the files at the pin) and each is now classed as absent, partly present, present outside the proposed modules or not applicable in Section 9 Q9.a (c); the read timeout and the connect retry are in Section 5 Q5.t.

### Q1.f Differences from the research input

The input is a hint only and is not cited. Differences found while re-verifying: (1) GitHub Packages API: the input recorded HTTP 403 (a token lacking a scope); without a credential the answer is 401, and the public page gives "0 packages" with working controls, a stronger result than the input had. (2) Mozilla Maven: directory probes for the candidate groups are not evidence of absence, because the registry returns 404 for an existing directory too; only the metadata-file probes carry weight. (3) The input's provenance wording is a flat "correction"; this analysis says "partly correct" because ownership transfer is documented while a purchase is neither described nor excluded. (4) JitPack: the builds API shows the vendored commit as "ok", which the input did not discuss (note above). The pin, the ahead/behind counts (11 and 15032) and the merge base agree with the input.
## 2. Modules and dependency graph

Assumed consumption (flagged in I1b, revisited in I2a): this section and Section 3 assume the **JVM variants** of the Kotlin Multiplatform project dependencies. `:mail:common` and `:mail:protocols:imap` are plain JVM modules [C-037, C-038], and every KMP module they depend on declares both an `android` and a `jvm` target [C-046]. Section 4 (I2a) keeps the Android variants live as consumption mode M2 (with its minSdk and compileSdk conflicts), so Section 3 Q3.b now holds the completed Android-variant licence table; the dependency table below remains the JVM-variant closure, and the Android additions are the three coordinates named in Q3.b with their POM-walk closure.

Pattern: `MailTransport` is an anti-corruption layer between the engine and the mail library (the role DES-MODERNIZATION-009 gives it). The two columns "behind the port" and "leaks past the port" below measure how much of the library surface the layer actually contains today.

Upstream status (applies to every row): the mail modules sit in the "Legacy Modules" group of upstream's own architecture document [C-080], which describes legacy code as "gradually being migrated" [C-081]. Types that are `internal`, `suspend` or in a KMP module are called out in the Notes column.

### Q2.a Recount of the 14 files

Command: `grep -rln "com\.fsck\.k9" app/src/main/java | grep -v /mail/transport/ | wc -l` run at the start commit gives **14**, the number REQ-MODERNIZATION-019 tables (8 message/MIME model, 6 transport). Difference from 14: none. Rows below follow REQ-019's order.

Definition used in the "behind / leaks" column: **behind the port** = the class is an implementation detail of the transport adapter side (only the adapter or its own pair reaches it); **leaks past the port** = k9 types sit in app code on the engine side of the port (message building, bootstrap, DI, preferences). "Reach" says whether code outside `mail/` can touch the file: *inside mail/* means every caller is in package `mail`; *outside mail/* means a class outside it uses the file or the file is itself outside it.

Whether the upstream APPEND transmits the internal date is a Section 5 question |

Totals for Section 11 (counted at the start commit): **behind the port: 3 files** (9, 10, 11; 15 import lines) and **leaks past the port: 11 files** (1-8, 12, 13, 14; 30 import lines; 5 of them reach only inside `mail/`, 6 reach outside it). Related problems the table shows: (a) error handling that depends on exception-message wording (the adapter compares `getMessage()` with the text "Unable to get IMAP prefix", `K9MailTransport.java:164,181,212,238,266,395`; analysed in Section 5, Q5.p), and (b) `BackupImapStore`, a mostly dead class with live static methods (row 9). Recount of the import lines: `grep -rhE '^import (static )?com\.fsck\.k9' app/src/main/java` gives **62 import lines in 16 files and 31 distinct imported names** (45 lines in the 14 files above, 17 lines in the 3 adapter files `K9MailTransport`, `MailMessageHandle`, `BackupFolderHandle`); the gate's own count was 61 and 33: 61 is the non-static import lines only (the 62 include the one static import, `MessageGenerator.java:33`), and 33 is the number of distinct `com.fsck.k9...` strings found anywhere in the sources, which adds two names that occur only in Javadoc (`com.fsck.k9.mail.*` at `K9MailTransport.java:69` and `com.fsck.k9.mail.FetchProfile.Item` at `FetchSpec.java:21`) to the 31 imported names; a difference of method, not of data (Section 11, Q11.a gives both counts again). 17 test files import `com.fsck.k9` (49 import lines).

### Q2.d The four legacy libraries

The app does not declare these four itself (`app/build.gradle:131` declares only the project `:k9mail-vendored`), but it imports two of them directly through the vendored module's `api` declarations: `org.apache.commons.io.IOUtils` in `mail/Attachment.java:13` and `mail/MessageConverter.java:40`, and `org.apache.james.mime4j.codec.EncoderUtil` in `utils/Sanitizer.java:3`. Upstream declares commons-io and mime4j as `implementation` (not `api`) [C-037], so after a migration the app would have to declare both itself (both classes exist in the upstream versions: `EncoderUtil` in apache-mime4j-core 0.8.15, `IOUtils` in commons-io 2.22.0, checked in the jars).

| apache-mime4j (core, dom) | 0.8.11 (`k9mail-vendored/build.gradle:100,102`) | 0.8.15 | **Retained** as a library (upstream uses mime4j-core and -dom in `:mail:common`); version **replaced** 0.8.11 to 0.8.15, and the app must declare mime4j-core itself for `EncoderUtil` | C-083, C-087, C-037 |
| jzlib | 1.0.7 (`k9mail-vendored/build.gradle:109`) | 1.0.7 | **Retained**, same version; used only by `:mail:protocols:imap` (compression) and not visible to the app | C-085, C-038 |

New to the app's closure (not in `:k9mail-vendored`): okio, moshi, httpclient5 with httpcore5 and httpcore5-h2 and slf4j-api, koin, kotlinx-coroutines/datetime/collections-immutable/serialization, kotlin-stdlib 2.4.x, and the two MPL-2.0 TMC components (Section 3 lists them with licences; Section 8 counts them).
## 3. Licence

Variant assumed: the **JVM variants** of the KMP project dependencies (see Section 2). The Android-variant additions are completed in Q3.b (the I2a revisit): Section 4 keeps that mode live, and every row there is "yes".

Private-app note: the app is not distributed. This affects *when* attribution and source-offer obligations trigger (on distribution), not the compatibility verdicts, which are about combining the licences.

### Q3.a Components and verdicts

Identifier source: *LICENSE opened* = the licence file at the pinned commit was read; *POM only* = the licence name and URL come from the POM that declares them (parent chain followed; the declaring POM is cited); *POM + licence text opened* = a licence text or file header was also read. Compatible = may be combined into an Apache-2.0 application. Every row below is **yes**; there is no non-"yes" row in the JVM set.

### Q3.b Android-variant additions (Section 4 keeps this mode live; closed in I2a)

Section 4 keeps the Android-variant consumption mode live (the PO option "raise minSdk to 24" makes it the natural mode), so the licence closure of its additions is completed here. `androidMain` adds `androidx.core:core-ktx` 1.19.1, `io.insert-koin:koin-android` 4.2.2 and `org.jetbrains.kotlinx:kotlinx-coroutines-android` 1.11.0 [C-048]. Method: POM walk from those three coordinates (compile and runtime scope, optional dependencies skipped, the highest declared version of each module wins, a version of the form `[x]` read as `x`); it reaches **72 modules**. The walk is POM-based, not Gradle-resolved: Gradle metadata files, constraints and exclusions were not applied, so the module set is an upper bound and the versions are the highest declared. Licence identifiers are from each module's own POM; one module (listenablefuture) inherits it from its parent POM, which is cited. AAR contents were listed by entry name only (nothing executed): 19 of the 44 AARs carry a `META-INF/androidx/.../LICENSE.txt`, and none carries a NOTICE entry.

The two entries that were open in I1b are closed: `androidx.appcompat:appcompat-resources` (the walk had met the range `[1.7.1]`) was fetched at 1.7.1 and declares Apache-2.0 [C-148]; `com.google.guava:listenablefuture:1.0` has no `<licenses>` in its own POM [C-149] but names the parent `com.google.guava:guava-parent:26.0-android`, whose POM declares "The Apache Software License, Version 2.0" [C-150]. Overlap with Q3.a: modules that also appear in the JVM set (koin-core, the stately artifacts, the kotlinx artifacts, annotations) are listed again at the version this walk found, and share their citation where the POM is the same. Three AAR-metadata facts from this step are used in Section 4: androidx.core and core-ktx 1.19.1 declare `minSdkVersion` 23 and `minCompileSdk` 37; activity 1.12.4 declares 23 and 36; koin-android 4.2.2 declares 23 [C-216, C-217, C-218, C-219].

### Q3.c MPL-2.0 items and file-level obligations

`net.thunderbird.components.core:outcome`, `net.thunderbird.components.core.logging:core` and the BOM are MPL-2.0 [C-100]; the components repository calls itself a work in progress, not ready for production use [C-109]. MPL-2.0 is a file-level (weak) copyleft licence. Obligations from the licence text [C-107]: (3.4) the licence notices in covered files may not be removed or altered; (3.1) if covered source is distributed, it must be under MPL-2.0 and recipients told how to obtain the licence; (3.2) if an executable containing covered software is distributed, the source of the covered files must be made available and recipients told how, while the executable as a whole may be under other terms; (3.3) a Larger Work, defined as a combination with other material in separate files [C-108], may be distributed under terms of your choice provided the MPL requirements for the covered files are met. Verdict: compatible with an Apache-2.0 application as a separately-filed dependency (yes), with obligations that bite only on distribution of the app or of modified covered files. The Larger Work clause (3.3) is the one that makes the combination permitted.

### Q3.d Attribution obligations that would replace `k9mail-vendored/NOTICE`

Today `k9mail-vendored/NOTICE:31-35` reproduces the original K-9 NOTICE (three lines) and `k9mail-vendored/LICENSE` holds the Apache text. A migration would replace those with:

1. The Apache-2.0 text (section 4(a)) for the upstream sources and the Apache-licensed binaries [C-102].
2. The upstream NOTICE text, section 4(d): "K-9 Mail / Copyright 2008-2016, K-9 Mail Developers / Copyright 2005-2016, The Android Open Source Project" [C-103]. This equals the lines the vendored NOTICE already carries.
3. The NOTICE text of each Apache binary that ships one: Apache James Mime4j Core and DOM (2004-2025), Apache Commons IO (2002-2026), Apache HttpClient (1999-2021), Apache HttpComponents Core HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2 (2005-2021), each "includes software developed at The Apache Software Foundation" [C-128, C-131, C-134, C-135]. Mime4j and commons-io are already in the vendored closure, their NOTICE text is not in `k9mail-vendored/NOTICE`; httpclient5 and httpcore5 are new.
4. If upstream source files are vendored: keep the third-party Apache-2.0 headers in seven files (`filter/Hex.kt`, `filter/Base64.java`, `filter/Base64OutputStream.java` from the Apache Software Foundation [C-104]; `helper/Rfc822Token.java`, `helper/Rfc822Tokenizer.java` from the Android Open Source Project and `helper/Utf8.kt` from Square [C-105]; `store/imap/ImapUtility.kt` from the Android Open Source Project [C-106]) and, for modified files, the section 4(b) modification notice. A scan of the in-scope source found no GPL, LGPL, MPL, EPL, CDDL or Creative Commons terms.
5. jzlib (BSD-3-Clause) and jutf7 (MIT) notices, and slf4j-api (MIT): these licences require the copyright and permission notices to accompany copies or binary documentation; jzlib and jutf7 are already in the vendored closure with no notice file for them.
6. MPL-2.0 (Q3.c) for the two TMC components: notices kept in covered files, source-availability statement if the app were ever distributed.

### Q3.e NOTICE-defect finding (REQ-MODERNIZATION-019)

REQ-019 reports that `k9mail-vendored/NOTICE` lists mime4j 0.7.2 and commons-io 2.4 while the module builds 0.8.11 and 2.16.1. What the files say: `k9mail-vendored/NOTICE:19-21` states that the dependency coordinates were "migrated from jcenter ... to mavenCentral equivalents at identical versions (apache-mime4j 0.7.2, commons-io 2.4, jutf7 1.0.0, jzlib 1.0.7, support-annotations -> androidx.annotation:1.7.1)", and the file's capture date is 2026-06-03 (`NOTICE:7`); `k9mail-vendored/build.gradle:16-19` records that mime4j was later bumped to 0.8.11 and commons-io to 2.16.1 per U-058. Git history: the NOTICE was last written by the U-027 capture commit on 2026-06-03; the bump commit of 2026-06-24 (U-058) did not touch it. **Classification: a historical record that became stale, not an error when written.** It describes the capture-time migration (original versions at the capture), but it carries no statement that the versions are current, so a reader can take it as the present state. It is not a licence-compliance defect: both libraries are Apache-2.0 at either version, and the NOTICE text that Apache section 4(d) needs (lines 31-35) is intact. The NOTICE was not edited.
## 4. Platform requirements

Scope. Upstream publishes no mail module (Section 1), so every consumption mode starts from upstream source. The two mail modules are plain JVM modules and the six KMP modules they need declare both an `android` and a `jvm` target [C-003, C-222, C-223]. Three modes are therefore distinguished in every upstream cell below: **M1 JVM variants** (the `jvm` targets of the KMP modules plus the two JVM mail modules, built from upstream source in this repository's build, for example as an included build or a submodule); **M2 Android variants** (the `android` targets, plus the `androidMain` dependencies of Section 3 Q3.b); **M3 source inclusion** (the needed upstream source files copied into a module of this repository and compiled with this app's own toolchain). The upstream build was not run (experiment (c) is "not run", D2), so what a mode asks of the toolchain is read from upstream's build files, not tested. Values for this app and for the target requirements were re-opened at the cited lines on 2026-11-13.

### Q4.a Consumption modes and which remain viable

| Mode | What is consumed | Floors that bind | Viable? |
|------|------------------|------------------|---------|
| M1 JVM variants | `:mail:common`, `:mail:protocols:imap` (plain JVM) and the `jvm` targets of the six KMP modules, built with upstream's build logic | no `minSdk` or `compileSdk` is declared by the plain-JVM plugin [C-003]; the KMP convention plugin still applies the Android multiplatform plugin to every KMP module [C-223], so evaluating upstream's build asks for the AGP row; bytecode 17 [C-220, C-224] | **viable**: no declared floor above 23. Not measured: the minimum Android API of upstream's third-party libraries (okio, moshi, httpclient5, koin, kotlinx); the mail modules call httpclient5 `DefaultHostnameVerifier` [C-240], analysed in Section 7 |
| M2 Android variants | the `android` targets of the six KMP modules plus `core-ktx`, `koin-android`, `kotlinx-coroutines-android` and their AndroidX closure | minSdk 24 and compileSdk 37 for the KMP Android targets [C-221, C-222]; AAR metadata of the Android set (minCompileSdk 37 for androidx.core 1.19.1 [C-216, C-217], 36 for activity 1.12.4 [C-218]); AGP and Gradle of upstream's build | **viable** as a technical mode; **conflicts** with minSdk 23 and with an unspecified compileSdk (rows below). The PO option "raise minSdk to 24" is what makes it the natural mode |
| M3 source inclusion | the needed upstream files (Java and Kotlin) compiled inside this repository | this app's own minSdk, compileSdk, Kotlin, AGP, Gradle, JDK; upstream's Kotlin version for the language level of the Kotlin files [C-228] | **viable**: nothing of upstream's platform settings binds; the cost is file count and licence headers (Sections 3 and 9) |
| none | - | - | **not the case**: M1, M2 and M3 each remain viable; Section 4 excludes no mode |

### Q4.b Platform table

Verdict rule for minSdk: "conflict" if any viable consumption mode conflicts, naming the mode. "Gating" means Part B cannot start until the conflict is resolved under every viable mode (Recommendation Logic, predicate G). Targets quote the owning requirement; REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 and -016 set "latest stable at the implementing story's planning date" with a 2026-11-12 snapshot (`REQ-MODERNIZATION-015:84-86`, `-016:35-36`), so a "no conflict" below means upstream's value is met by the snapshot.

| Item | Upstream at pin (cited; mode) | Target (owning REQ file:line) | App now (file:line) | Verdict | Resolution options / PO decision needed |
|------|-------------------------------|-------------------------------|---------------------|---------|------------------------------------------|
| minSdk | **M2:** 24 for the KMP Android targets [C-221, C-222]; the Android set adds modules declaring 23 (androidx.core and core-ktx 1.19.1 [C-216, C-217], activity 1.12.4 [C-218], koin-android 4.2.2 [C-219]; the MPL-2.0 components `outcome` and `logging:core` declare 23 for their Android targets [C-241]). **M1 and the `:mail:*` modules:** no minSdk (plain JVM plugin, no Android plugin) [C-003]. **M3:** the app's own | 23 (`REQ-MODERNIZATION-015:34`, `:113`) | 21 (`app/build.gradle:24`); vendored module 21 (`k9mail-vendored/build.gradle:54`) | **conflict** (mode M2: 24 is above 23). M1 and M3 declare no floor; the API-level check found 0 counted hits (Q4.c) | **Non-gating**: M1 and M3 do not conflict as measured. Options: (a) raise minSdk to 24, which needs a change record against REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 AC-5 because the approved value is 23; (b) keep 23 and use M1 or M3; (c) no-go. **PO decision needed.** This analysis does not change 23. Reopen if Section 7 shows that M1 and M3 both need an API above 23 |

### Q4.c API-level check of the JVM-only mail modules (minSdk 23)

"No minSdk" does not mean "runs at API 23": Android lint does not check plain JVM modules (too strong: corrected by the dated note in Q4.e and in Q7.g), so the Java APIs they call were searched. Command at the pin, with the pattern file kept in scratch (the plan's alternation with `\b(List|Map|Set)\.of\(` anchored and `java\.nio\.file` included): `git -C <scratch>/tb grep -nE -f <scratch>/q4pat.re ac1f0e85... -- <source sets>`. Source sets searched (paths relative to the repository root): `mail/common/src/main`, `mail/protocols/imap/src/main`, `core/common/src/commonMain`, `core/common/src/commonJvmMain`, `core/common/src/jvmMain`, `core/common/src/androidMain`, `legacy/logging/src/commonMain`, `feature/account/api/src/commonMain`, `feature/mail/folder/api/src/commonMain`, `core/architecture/api/src/commonMain`, `feature/mail/account/api/src/commonMain` (test source sets excluded). The patterns: `java.time`, `java.nio.file`, `java.util.Optional`, `java.util.stream`, `List.of(`/`Map.of(`/`Set.of(`, `.repeat(`, `.isBlank(`, `.strip(`.

| Hit | Language and receiver | Counted? | API level |
|-----|-----------------------|----------|-----------|
| `core/common/.../StringExtension.kt:13` `" ".repeat(size)` [C-236] | Kotlin file, receiver is a Kotlin `String`: Kotlin stdlib `String.repeat` | no | none needed |
| `mail/common/.../ServerSettings.kt:20` `username.isBlank()` and `:21` the `isBlank()` and `isNullOrBlank()` calls on `username` and `password` [C-237] | Kotlin file, Kotlin `String` and `String?` receivers: Kotlin stdlib | no | none needed |

Result: **3 hits, 0 counted**; no `java.time`, `java.nio.file`, `Optional`, stream or `List.of` use in the searched sets. Supplemental search (not part of the plan pattern, run so that a clean result is not over-read): `java.util.function`, `java.util.Base64`, `java.util.concurrent`, `computeIfAbsent`, `getOrDefault`, `removeIf`, `.stream(`, `CompletableFuture`, `java.nio.`, `StandardCharsets`, `Objects.` found only `Objects.requireNonNull` (API 19) [C-238], `StandardCharsets` (API 19) [C-239] and `java.nio.charset`/`ByteBuffer` (API 9 or lower), none above 23. This is a text search, not a bytecode API scan: it does not cover APIs reached through third-party libraries (core-library desugaring is not enabled in this repository: `grep -n "desugar\|coreLibrary" app/build.gradle k9mail-vendored/build.gradle` is empty). The mail modules import no `kotlinx.datetime`, koin or `java.time`; they import okio, moshi and httpclient5 (import grep).

### Q4.d Verdicts, gating and viable modes

- **minSdk: conflict** (rule: any viable mode conflicts; the conflicting mode is M2, 24 against 23). **Non-gating**: M1 and M3 carry no floor above 23 as measured. Viable modes: M1, M2, M3 (none is excluded). PO decision needed (options in the table); the approved value 23 is not changed here.
- **compileSdk: conflict (unresolved: target not specified)**, mode M2 only; **non-gating** by D4.5. PO decision needed.
- **Kotlin, kotlinx-coroutines, AGP, Gradle: no conflict** against the REQ-015/016 targets; each carries a sequencing condition (Part B after REQ-015 and REQ-016). **JVM target and JDK to build: no conflict.**
- **Gating conflicts: none.** Predicate G (Recommendation Logic) is therefore false as measured here. Plan interpretation: G is read as "Part B cannot start until resolved under every viable mode", and M1 and M3 start without resolving either conflict.
- Conflicts count: 2 of 8 rows (minSdk, compileSdk), both limited to M2.

### Q4.e Revisit of Sections 2 and 3 (flagged in I1b)

Branch taken: **Section 4 keeps the Android variants live** (M2 is viable, with the conflicts above). Section 3 Q3.b therefore now holds the completed Android-variant licence table (72 modules, all "yes") and its two open entries (`appcompat-resources`, `listenablefuture`) are closed; Section 2's dependency table remains the JVM-variant closure, with a one-line cross-reference added to its introduction. The JVM-set verdicts of Sections 2 and 3 are unchanged.

Dated note (2026-11-13, added in I2b; the Section 4 text above is unchanged). (1) The reopen condition in the minSdk row of Q4.b was checked in Section 7 (Q7.e): the TLS and socket classes that M1 and M3 would bring in from the mail modules and `:core:common` contain no API above 23 by the pattern search, and the only API 24 use in the TLS path (`SNIHostName` and `SSLParameters.serverNames`) is in `legacy/core/.../DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.kt`, a class outside the proposed module set that the app does not need because it keeps its own guarded factory. M1 and M3 therefore do not both need an API above 23, and the minSdk verdict stands (conflict in mode M2 only, non-gating). (2) Correction to Q4.c: the sentence "Android lint does not check plain JVM modules" is too strong. Both mail modules apply the standalone Android lint plugin (`mail/common/build.gradle.kts:3`, `mail/protocols/imap/build.gradle.kts:3`, plugin id at `gradle/libs.versions.toml:138`) [C-037, C-038, C-361]. Which minSdk that lint run assumes for a JVM module was not examined, so the text search of Q4.c remains the only API-level evidence and its result (3 hits, 0 counted) is unchanged.

## 5. API mapping

Scope. Each operation is read at three places: this app's call (file:line in this repository), the vendored implementation (`k9mail-vendored/`) and upstream at the pin. "Java-callable" is decided from the declaration: a `suspend` modifier, `internal` visibility, an interface where the app subclasses a class, or a package-private class forces Kotlin or a fork; reaching an `internal` member through name mangling would count as unsupported, not "yes". The upstream build was not run, so no compile proof exists; every "yes" and "via shim" rests on a cited public declaration. Primary sources win over the research input; contradictions with it are collected in Q5.f.

### Q5.a Operations

| Operation | App call today (file:line) | Replacement call at pin (cited) | Behavioural difference | Java-callable without coroutines (yes / no / via shim + evidence) | Achievable without forking (yes / needs fork of ...) |
|-----------|----------------------------|---------------------------------|------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------|
| 1. Connect / login | `new BackupImapStoreDelegate(context, config.storeUri, factory)` `K9MailTransport.java:121-122` (vendored `ImapStore(StoreConfig, TrustedSocketFactory, ConnectivityManager)` decodes the store URI, `ImapStore.java:75-81`); the connection opens at the first operation, `store.checkSettings()` `K9MailTransport.java:158` (vendored `ImapStore.java:306-316`); library bootstrap `K9MailLib.setDebugStatus` `App.java:260` | `ImapStore.create(serverSettings, config, trustedSocketFactory, oauthTokenProvider)` [C-245]; `checkSettings()` [C-242, C-246]; `ServerSettings` [C-249]; `ConnectionSecurity` [C-250]; `ImapStoreConfig` with four members [C-078]; `ImapClientInfo(appName, appVersion)` [C-251]; token provider interface [C-253]; bootstrap: `Log.logger` is a `lateinit var` on a `@Discouraged` object [C-079], plus `K9MailLib.setDebugStatus` [C-065] | (1) No store URI: the adapter builds `ServerSettings` from the app's URI. (2) `ConnectivityManager` and `NetworkType` are gone; compression and the sending of client information are settings extras that **default to true** [C-252], whereas the app returns `useCompression` false (`BackupStoreConfig.java:21`), so the adapter must set both extras explicitly to keep the wire behaviour. (3) Upstream `checkSettings` rethrows any exception [C-246]: an `IOException` is no longer wrapped as `MessagingException("Unable to connect")` (vendored `ImapStore.java:313-315`). (4) `autoconfigureFolders` (vendored `ImapStore.java:311`) has no counterpart. (5) `Log.logger` must be assigned before any mail call or the first log call fails (`lateinit`). (6) Credentials/token go through `OAuth2TokenProvider` (Section 6). (7) **Read timeout:** the vendored fork waits up to 120000 ms for each socket read (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/store/RemoteStore.java:21`, fork commit [C-448]); the pin uses 60000 ms [C-433] and offers no way to set it through `ImapStore.create(...)` [C-245, C-437] (Q5.t). (8) **Connect retry:** the fork retries a failed connect per address with backoff inside one 30 s budget [C-449]; the pin tries each address once with the full 30 s each [C-435] (Q5.t). (9) **Connection security:** there is no store URI at the pin, so the adapter sets `ConnectionSecurity` itself, and the library does not refuse to send a password on `NONE` (Q7.h) | **yes**: `ImapStore.Companion.create(...)` (the companion is not `@JvmStatic`, so Java calls it through `Companion`) [C-071]; `ServerSettings` has `@JvmOverloads` [C-249]; `checkSettings()` is not `suspend` [C-242] | **yes** for the calls; the 120 s read timeout of difference (7) is **not** achievable without a fork: **needs fork of** `NetworkTimeouts.kt`, one line [C-433]; non-fork alternatives and their cost: Q5.t |

(4) The text "Did not find message count during open" and "Unable to get IMAP prefix" are still thrown (Q5.p). (5) `closeAllConnections()` is public and also bumps the connection generation so connections in use are not returned to the pool [C-248], matching the intent of the BUG-013 pool reset | **yes**: all are plain functions of public interfaces [C-072] | **yes** |

Library-wide answer to "does the library force Kotlin or coroutines": **only the fetch of rows 5 and 8 does** (`ImapFolder.fetch`, `suspend`; the "via shim" answer is argued from the declaration, not compiled); every other call used by the app is a plain public function of a public interface or class. Fork-dependent rows: 3 (APPEND internal date) and 4 (UID SEARCH by arbitrary header and `SENTSINCE`); row 5's date-only fetch is **not** fork-dependent. Row 1 carries one further fork-dependent setting, the 120 s read timeout (Q5.t); rows 2, 5, 6, 7 and 8 are "yes" without qualification.

### Q5.b The three capability tests (vendored source and upstream at the pin)

Story P4 names three things the fork has and upstream lacks. Each is tested on both sides with the plan's commands.

**Test 1: APPEND with an explicit internal date.** Vendored: the command carries a date-time fed from `message.getInternalDate()` (current time if null) at `k9mail-vendored/.../store/imap/ImapFolder.java:1174-1177`; the app sets the date at `HeaderGenerator.java:56`; the fork commit "Set internal date on APPEND" is in the fork-only list of Section 1 [C-034], so the change never reached upstream (no history pickaxe was run, as planned). Upstream: `git grep -n APPEND ac1f0e85... -- mail/protocols/imap/src/main` finds one command format, `"APPEND %s (%s) {%d}"` with folder, flags and size [C-264]; `git grep -n -i internalDate ... -- mail/protocols/imap/src/main` finds only fetch-side uses (`INTERNALDATE` requested in ENVELOPE [C-277], and `message.internalDate = ...` when parsing a fetch [C-265]); the public `ImapFolder` has no member that sends a raw command [C-072]; the connection that could is `internal` [C-274]. **Result: not refuted (story P4 holds for this item): the format carries no date-time argument. Verdict: needs fork of `RealImapFolder.kt` (one command string).**

Non-fork alternatives and their cost (plan note O13):

1. *Accept the server-assigned internal date.* The call works unchanged (row 3). Costs: it contradicts `REQ-MODERNIZATION-019:84` (the byte-comparable list names internal date) and AC-B5 (`REQ-MODERNIZATION-019:106`, "unchanged ... internal date") as written; EPIC-MODERNIZATION-006 success criterion 9 (`EPIC-MODERNIZATION-006:107-109`) lists "message content, headers, MIME parts, and folder naming" and does not name internal date, so the criterion survives and the REQ text would need a change record. Restore ordering is unaffected: the `max` trimming sorts by the `Date` header (`MessageComparator`, `K9MailTransport.java:769-775`). Date-bounded searches by internal date would differ for newly appended messages, but production passes `since = null` (`RestoreWorker.kt:243,249`). Whether mail clients that order by internal date would show a different order was not measured here (it would be settled by the live-account run, AC-B7). A mailbox would hold two populations: messages appended before the migration (internal date = sent date) and after it (append time).
2. *Provide the raw command from the app.* Not possible without the fork: the connection type is `internal` [C-274] and `RealImapFolder` is `internal` [C-077]; calling `internal` members through mangled names counts as unsupported.
3. *Copy the patched upstream file into the app* is the fork itself, counted once under predicate F.

**Test 2: UID SEARCH on an arbitrary header, with the four live criteria.** Vendored: `HEADER X-SMSSYNC-DATATYPE "<type>"`, `UNDELETED`, `SENTSINCE <date>`, `FLAGGED` are one command string built at `K9MailTransport.java:749-758` and run through the `protected` `executeSimpleCommand` (`ImapFolder.java:117`) from an `ImapSearcher` (`:716-721`); the fork commit that made those members reachable is [C-036]. Upstream: `git grep -nE "HEADER|UNDELETED|SENTSINCE|FLAGGED|executeSimpleCommand|ImapSearcher" ac1f0e85... -- mail/protocols/imap/src/main` finds no `UNDELETED`, no `SENTSINCE`, no `ImapSearcher`; `HEADER` is used once, for `MESSAGE-ID` only [C-276]; `FLAGGED` appears as a flag term [C-271]; `executeSimpleCommand` is private [C-273] and the builder class is package-private [C-269]. The public `search` emits only `TEXT` or the five-field OR plus flag terms [C-270, C-254].

| Criterion | Vendored (live) | Public API at the pin | Honoured without a fork? |
|-----------|-----------------|-----------------------|--------------------------|
| `HEADER X-SMSSYNC-DATATYPE "<type>"` | in the query, `K9MailTransport.java:752-753` | none (header search only for `MESSAGE-ID`) [C-276] | no; alternatives below |
| `UNDELETED` | `K9MailTransport.java:754` | `forbiddenFlags = {DELETED}` emits `NOT DELETED` [C-271] | yes (equivalent) |
| `SENTSINCE <date>` | `K9MailTransport.java:755` (production passes `since = null`, `RestoreWorker.kt:243,249`) | `SINCE` on internal date only [C-275] | no for the server-side filter; yes by a client-side filter on `getSentDate()` after an ENVELOPE fetch (exact) |
| `FLAGGED` | `K9MailTransport.java:756` (live: `restoreOnlyStarred`) | `requiredFlags = {FLAGGED}` [C-271] | yes |
| `max` trimming | `K9MailTransport.java:727-744`: DATE fetch, sort by `getSentDate()`, then reverse | ENVELOPE fetch (test 3) then the same sort and trim in app code | yes, once the candidate set is right |
| `getUid` equivalent | `msg.getUid()` `K9MailTransport.java:230` | `Message.getUid()` [C-267] on `ImapMessage` [C-268] | yes |

**Result: not refuted: no public, non-`internal` API accepts the header criterion or SENTSINCE or a raw command. Verdict: needs fork of `UidSearchCommandBuilder.java` and the `search` plumbing (`RealImapFolder.kt:1271-1300`, and `ImapFolder.kt:62-68` if a criteria parameter is added).** Non-fork alternatives:

1. *`TEXT` search plus a client-side filter:* `search(<text>, requiredFlags = {FLAGGED} if starred, forbiddenFlags = {DELETED}, performFullTextSearch = true)` returns a candidate superset (how a server matches `TEXT` against a header line is server-defined and was not tested). The `X-smssync-datatype` header is not in the ENVELOPE list [C-277], so each candidate needs a full `BODY.PEEK[]` fetch [C-278] to be filtered, attachments of MMS candidates included: cost is one body download per candidate where today one search command returns only matches. `SENTSINCE` is then filtered client-side from the `Date` header; `UNDELETED` and `FLAGGED` are honoured by the flag arguments; `max` trimming is unchanged.
2. *Per-datatype folders without a header search:* SMS and MMS use the same preference key and default folder name, `DataType.java:16-17` (`IMAP_FOLDER`, `Defaults.SMS_FOLDER` "SMS"), and MMS has no restore preference (`DataType.java:17`, restore key `null`), so a restore that reads the SMS folder (`RestoreWorker.kt:243`) relies on the header search to skip the MMS messages stored beside the SMS. A per-type layout changes where existing users' messages live (EPIC success criterion 9 names folder naming) and still needs a migration for mailboxes already written. Not equivalent without that migration.
3. *Range search* `getMessages(start, end, earliestDate, listener)` [C-275]: honours `NOT DELETED` and an internal-date `SINCE`, but not the datatype or `FLAGGED` criteria, so it is not a substitute.

**Test 3: the date-only fetch (`FetchProfile.Item.DATE`, fork-only).** (i) *Does upstream's ENVELOPE header list contain `date`?* Yes: `BODY.PEEK[HEADER.FIELDS (date subject from content-type to cc bcc reply-to message-id references in-reply-to list-post list-unsubscribe sender ...)]` [C-277]. (ii) *Is the only live consumer `MessageComparator` through `getSentDate()`?* Yes. The three `Item.DATE` sites: `K9MailTransport.java:731` (live: the `max` trimming of `getMessagesInternal`, then `sort(msgs, MessageComparator.INSTANCE)` `:735`, comparator reading `getSentDate()` `:769-775`); `K9MailTransport.java:258` (the port method `fetch(..., ENVELOPE_DATE)`, which has no production caller: only `K9MailTransportTest.java:315` and `K9MailTransportHandleResolutionTest.java:81` use it); `BackupImapStore.java:181` (the legacy class, whose only live members are its static validators; it sorts with its own `MessageComparator`, `BackupImapStore.java:186`). Hits at the last two sites are not refutations. The vendored header-only fetches are `ENVELOPE` (`ImapFolder.java:685-689`) and `DATE` (`:708-709`, only `date`); the fork commit is [C-035]. (iii) *Does upstream `MimeMessage.getSentDate()` parse the `Date` header?* Yes [C-281]. **Result: all three hold: achievable without forking: yes (ENVELOPE superset).** Difference per 100-UID window (`FETCH_WINDOW_SIZE` is 100 in both trees [C-280]): the vendored `DATE` item requests one header field, `date`; the ENVELOPE item requests, per message, `INTERNALDATE`, `RFC822.SIZE` and 16 header field names (date, subject, from, content-type, to, cc, bcc, reply-to, message-id, references, in-reply-to, list-post, list-unsubscribe, sender and two client-specific headers) instead of the one field `date`: 15 further field names and two further data items. A full-body fetch is not the substitute and is not proposed. The port maps `FetchSpec.ENVELOPE_DATE` to `Item.ENVELOPE` (adapter change only). **Story P4 item 3 mis-states the gap**: it is not a fetch of the `X-smssync-*` headers (neither the vendored header-only fetches nor upstream's fetch them; the app reads them after a full-body fetch, `K9MailTransport.java:256,299` then `Headers.java:30-37`); the fork-only item is `Item.DATE`, which the research notes already identify.

### Q5.t Read timeout and connect retry (fork commits `013948a758` and `69eef8239b`)

Added in rework round 1 (IR1, 2026-11-13) after the code review (warning W2). Every statement was re-opened in this repository and in the clone at the pin. Where this text differs from the review, the primary source is followed and the difference is stated in the last paragraph.

**Vendored side.** The fork raised the socket read timeout from 60000 to 120000 ms in a one-line commit [C-448]. The value is `RemoteStore.SOCKET_READ_TIMEOUT` (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/store/RemoteStore.java:21`), copied into each `ImapConnection` by its public constructor (`ImapConnection.java:82-89`), the only constructor that `ImapStore.createImapConnection` calls (`ImapStore.java:371-373`), and applied with `socket.setSoTimeout` (`ImapConnection.java:229`). The commit has a subject line and no explanation, so why 120 s was chosen is not recorded. Nothing in this app sets a mail-socket timeout: a search of `app/src` for `setSoTimeout` and read-timeout settings finds one hit, `PinCertificateEnrollmentFlow.java:350` (10 s on the separate certificate-fetch socket of the enrolment screen), and neither socket factory sets one; the `*_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` preference keys are schedule settings.

**Where the app's long waits sit.** The read timeout bounds each blocking read, that is the longest silence from the server, not the length of a transfer. The app waits on the server in two long operations: APPEND of an MMS with attachments (the vendored code writes the literal, then reads responses until the tagged reply, `ImapFolder.java:1177-1194`; app call `K9MailTransport.java:201`) and the full-body FETCH of a restored message (`K9MailTransport.java:255-260,298-301`). How long a real server stays silent in either was not measured.

**Upstream at the pin, link by link.** (1) The value is 60000 [C-433]. (2) `RealImapConnection` is `internal` and takes the timeout as a constructor parameter with that default [C-434]. (3) `configureSocket()` applies it with `socket?.soTimeout = ...`, after the connect and again after the STARTTLS upgrade [C-436, C-440, C-356], so a timeout set by the supplied socket factory on its socket is overwritten. (4) `RealImapStore.createImapConnection` passes no timeout [C-437]. (5) `ImapStore.create(...)` has four parameters and none is a timeout [C-245]; `ServerSettings` has none [C-249]; `ImapStoreConfig` has four members and none is a timeout [C-078]. (6) `RealImapStore` is `internal open` [C-438], so the app cannot subclass it to override `createImapConnection`. Every link agrees with the code review: there is no public way to set the IMAP read timeout at the pin.

**Decision: keeping 120 s is not achievable without a fork.** The patch that mirrors the fork commit is one line in `NetworkTimeouts.kt` (the shared constant [C-433]) or the call in `RealImapStore.kt` [C-437]. The status of the setting is **needs fork of `NetworkTimeouts.kt`**. Non-fork alternatives and their cost, in the form used for the other fork rows (Q5.b):

1. *Accept 60 s.* No code. Cost: an APPEND reply or a FETCH read that stays silent for more than 60 s now fails on a connection the fork would have kept for up to 120 s; the failure is a read timeout inside the library call. Whether this app meets such silences with a real server is not measured (the live-account run of condition 14 would show it). It is a behaviour change from today, so it is a product owner decision (PO-13).
2. *An app-owned socket wrapper.* The library calls `setSoTimeout` on the socket object that the supplied factory returned [C-436], so a delegating subclass of the platform `SSLSocket` whose `setSoTimeout` ignores or raises the value would keep 120 s through the public `TrustedSocketFactory` [C-067, C-245]. This was not written, compiled or run. It needs a wrapper for the socket of the implicit-TLS path and for the socket of the STARTTLS upgrade [C-355, C-356], is app-owned code on the TLS path of Section 7, and its cost is a new class with tests.
3. *Patch `NetworkTimeouts.kt`* is the fork itself, counted once under predicate F.

**The connect retry, and where this text differs from the code review.** Fork commit `69eef8239b` [C-449] replaced the loop over the resolved addresses with up to 3 tries per address (the connect timeout of a try starts at 5 s and doubles), ordered by last attempt with IPv6 first, all inside one 30 s budget (vendored `ImapConnection.java:185-226` and the `ConnectableAddress` class from `:763`, `initialBackoff` and `maxTries` at `:765-766`). The code review read the pin as covering this ("`RealImapConnection.kt:154-166` loops over all addresses"). Reading the commit's pre-image and the pin together, that loop is what the fork replaced, not what it added: at the pin each address is tried once with the full 30 s connect timeout and the last `IOException` is thrown [C-435, C-355]. **The per-address retry, the backoff and the overall budget are absent at the pin**; only the try-every-address part is common. Nothing in the app, the port contract or the acceptance criteria depends on the retry (CNTR-MODERNIZATION-007 has no timeout clause: `grep -n -i timeout` on the contract returns no line), so it is recorded as a behaviour difference (Q5.a row 1, difference (8)), not as a fork row or a condition. Restoring it would patch a further file, `RealImapConnection.kt`; PO-13 offers that choice and this analysis does not propose it.

**Effect on counts.** The read timeout adds one fork-dependent setting to Section 5 (inside row 1) and one file to the patched upstream files, which become **2 to 4** (were 2 to 3): `RealImapFolder.kt` and `UidSearchCommandBuilder.java` (rows 3 and 4), `ImapFolder.kt` if a criteria parameter is added (row 4), and `NetworkTimeouts.kt` only if 120 s is kept by fork; with the timeout accepted or wrapped the count is 2 to 3. Predicate F was already true (rows 3 and 4); B, G, S, P and the selected row are unchanged. Decision: PO-13; proof: condition 16.

### Q5.p Port preservation (CNTR-MODERNIZATION-007)

Facts and verdicts only; no interface is proposed (that is Part B design). Verdict values, as used in both sub-tables: **preserved** = the backing call is usable as it is, apart from import renames; **preserved with adapter change** = the adapter's own logic must change (a shim, a broadened catch, a mapping, or absorbing a fork or a non-fork alternative) while every port signature and promise stays; **needs contract change (CR)** = something visible at the port or in the contract text cannot be kept as written. A port member whose backing call needs a fork is "preserved with adapter change" and cites the Section 5 operation row that says "needs fork"; the fork is counted once, under predicate F (gate note R2). The first sub-table has 9 rows, the second 5. "CNTR-007:n" is a line of `sdlc/artifacts/design/contracts/CNTR-MODERNIZATION-007-mail-transport.md`.

| Port member (MailTransport.java line) | CNTR-007 clause (line) | Upstream backing call at pin (cited) | Throwables upstream can raise (FQN) | Maps to | Verdict | Notes (contract-text drift) |

Exception-mapping verdicts: **preserved 2**, **preserved with adapter change 2**, **needs contract change (CR) 1** (P-2). Rows: 5.

**The three load-bearing checks.** (a) *XOAUTH2 status:* upstream exposes **no** XOAUTH2-specific type and **no** status [C-299, C-300]. The adapter cannot derive the real status: it is not on the exception chain. It could only synthesise a value: the app-supplied `OAuth2TokenProvider` [C-253] is told of each failure through `invalidateToken()` [C-300], and a constant `400` would make the engine refresh on failures the library already treated as permanent (the library retries once itself when the challenge status is 400 or unreadable [C-302]) and `-1` would stop the refresh the engine relies on. The exact `getStatus()` semantics therefore cannot be preserved: **P-2**. (b) *"Unable to get IMAP prefix":* still thrown with the same text [C-261]: preserved. (c) *Folder-not-ready text:* "Did not find message count during open" is still thrown [C-260]; `NONEXISTENT` still arrives through the server text inside the exception message [C-305]: preserved, text-based, Part B behaviour risk only.

**P items from Section 5** (non-selecting predicate P; route for each: a change record against CNTR-MODERNIZATION-007, raised through the change-record skill after U-062's QA and before any Part B design; neither is decided here):

### Q5.f Differences from the research input and from the story

The input is a hint only and is not cited. (1) **Story P4 item 3** ("fetch of the `X-smssync-*` headers") mis-states the gap: neither the vendored header-only fetches nor upstream's fetch those headers; the fork-only item is `FetchProfile.Item.DATE`, which the research notes already identify. The input and the primary sources agree here; the story summary is the one that is wrong. (2) The input dates the change that made `fetch` `suspend` as 2026-09-28; the clone gives commit 4b0e06b90f author date 2026-09-17 and committer date 2026-09-28 (`git show -s --format="%ad %cd" --date=short`), so the input used the committer date; story P5 ("September 2026") holds either way. (3) Additions the input does not state, found in the primary sources: `RealImapStore.checkSettings` no longer wraps `IOException`; `AddressHeaderBuilder` rejects an empty array where the vendored `setRecipients` removed the header; compression and client-info sending default to true at the pin; SMS and MMS share one folder, which makes the header search load-bearing for restore; the components repository's Android targets declare minSdk 23 and compileSdk 37 (Section 4). No other contradiction was found.
## 6. Authentication

Scope. This app reads and appends mail over IMAP only, so the IMAP connection class is read at the pin; the SMTP transport is out of scope. "Implemented" means a code path that sends the mechanism's authentication command. Statements about this repository and the vendored library were re-opened at the cited lines on 2026-11-13. No live server was contacted in Part A, so which mechanism a particular server (for example Gmail) would negotiate is not tested; it is a live-account check for Part B (REQ-MODERNIZATION-019 AC-B7).

### Q6.a Mechanisms at the pin

| Mechanism | Implemented (yes/no) | How selected | Citation |
|-----------|----------------------|--------------|----------|
| PLAIN (SASL `AUTHENTICATE PLAIN`) | **yes** | `AuthType.PLAIN` and the server advertises `AUTH=PLAIN`; if the SASL attempt fails with `AuthenticationFailedException` and the connection is still open, the `LOGIN` command is tried next. Without `AUTH=PLAIN` the `LOGIN` command is used directly unless the server advertises `LOGINDISABLED`; otherwise `MissingCapabilityException` | C-314, C-315, C-327 |
| LOGIN (IMAP `LOGIN` command) | **yes**, as a command used by `AuthType.PLAIN`; there is no `AuthType` constant named LOGIN at the pin | Only as the two PLAIN fallbacks above (after a failed SASL PLAIN; or when `AUTH=PLAIN` is absent and `LOGINDISABLED` is not advertised) | C-316, C-314, C-066 |
| CRAM-MD5 | **yes** | `AuthType.CRAM_MD5` and `AUTH=CRAM-MD5`; otherwise `MissingCapabilityException` | C-317, C-318, C-330 |
| EXTERNAL | **yes** | `AuthType.EXTERNAL` and `AUTH=EXTERNAL`; the client certificate comes from the socket factory through `clientCertificateAlias` (Section 7) | C-319, C-320 |
| XOAUTH2 | **yes** | `AuthType.XOAUTH2` selects the OAuth path (needs an `OAuth2TokenProvider` and the `SASL-IR` capability). Inside that path `AUTH=XOAUTH2` is used only when the server does **not** advertise `AUTH=OAUTHBEARER` | C-321, C-323, C-331, C-324 |
| OAUTHBEARER | **yes**, with no `AuthType` constant of its own | Negotiated inside the `XOAUTH2` path: preferred whenever the server advertises `AUTH=OAUTHBEARER` (RFC 7628 initial client response) | C-321, C-323, C-329, C-328, C-066 |
| Other mechanisms (SCRAM, NTLM, GSSAPI, DIGEST-MD5, ANONYMOUS) | **no** | None. The only `AUTH=` capabilities the IMAP module knows are the five above; `AuthType.NONE` (and anything else) ends in an exception. Search: `git grep -ni -e SCRAM -e NTLM -e GSSAPI -e DIGEST -e ANONYMOUS -e AUTH= -e AUTOMATIC` at the pin over `mail/common/src/main` and `mail/protocols/imap/src/main` returns 13 lines: the five capability constants, six lines in `Authentication.java` (the XOAUTH2 format and the MD5 inside CRAM-MD5), the OAUTHBEARER builder and a MIME `multipart/digest` check; none is another mechanism | C-327, C-322, C-066 |

### Q6.b How the mechanism is selected

1. **Configuration decides the branch.** `ServerSettings.authenticationType` becomes the connection's `authType` [C-249, C-332]; `authenticate()` runs a `when` on it [C-313]. There is no discovery across `AuthType` values and no ordered list of mechanisms to try: a type either finds its capability or fails.
2. **Server capability check inside the branch.** CRAM-MD5, PLAIN, EXTERNAL and the OAuth path each test the advertised capability first and throw `MissingCapabilityException` when it is absent [C-313, C-327].
3. **The only fallbacks.** PLAIN to LOGIN (two forms, Q6.a); XOAUTH2 type: `OAUTHBEARER` before `XOAUTH2`; and one retry with a fresh token (next item).
4. **Token retry.** On a negative response the library calls `invalidateToken()` and retries once with `getToken(...)`; a second failure is treated as permanent and thrown as `AuthenticationFailedException("Authentication failed", ...)` [C-300]. A negative response to PLAIN, LOGIN, CRAM-MD5 or EXTERNAL with no response code, or `AUTHENTICATIONFAILED`, also becomes `AuthenticationFailedException`; any other response code is rethrown as the negative response and the connection is closed [C-325].
5. **Credentials.** `ServerSettings.isMissingCredentials` needs only a user name for XOAUTH2 and a user name and password for the password types [C-249].

### Q6.c Token provider: interface versus implementation, and what the app supplies

- **Interface:** `OAuth2TokenProvider` in `:mail:common` with three members: `usernames` (a set), `getToken(timeoutMillis: Long): String` and `invalidateToken()`; the default timeout constant is 30000 ms; the documentation says that invalidating a token and then failing with a new one "should be treated as a permanent failure" [C-253]. The store entry point takes it as a nullable fourth parameter [C-245]; the connection requires it for `AuthType.XOAUTH2` [C-326, C-313]. All three members are plain (non-`suspend`) functions, so a Java class can implement the interface.
- **Implementation:** the only implementation at the pin, `RealOAuth2TokenProvider`, is in `:legacy:common`, an Android library built on AppAuth that depends on the legacy core, storage, UI and backend modules [C-333, C-334]. `:legacy:common` is not in the proposed module set (Section 2), so the implementation cannot be consumed with the mail modules.
- **What the app would have to supply:** its own `OAuth2TokenProvider`. The library calls `getToken` before each attempt and `invalidateToken()` after a negative response, then `getToken` once more [C-324, C-300]. The app's tokens live in `AuthPreferences` (`getOauth2Token()` `app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/preferences/AuthPreferences.java:145`) and are refreshed by `TokenRefresher.refreshOAuth2Token()` (`app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/auth/TokenRefresher.java:42`), which today runs in the worker after an `XOAuth2FailedException` with status 400 (`app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/service/BackupWorker.kt:442-446`). A provider would therefore have to return a refreshed token on the second `getToken` call, so the refresh moves behind `invalidateToken()`/`getToken` or the worker keeps refreshing before it builds the store. Which of the two is a Part B design decision; this analysis records only that the seam exists and what crosses it.
- **Failure typing (carried from Section 5, not re-decided here):** upstream has no XOAUTH2 failure type with a status; an XOAUTH2 failure arrives as a plain `AuthenticationFailedException` [C-299, C-300, C-302]. This is P-2 in Section 5 (CNTR-MODERNIZATION-007 `:222`). The challenge parser that reads the status uses Moshi for its JSON [C-335] and returns only a boolean [C-302]. **Security consequence of losing the status (added in rework round 1, security review L-1).** At the pin the OAuth branch is bounded: per connection open it asks the provider for a token at most twice and calls `invalidateToken()` at most twice, and it has no path to a password mechanism, because the branch is chosen by the configured `AuthType` (Q6.b) and the token is sent as a sensitive command [C-300, C-442, C-324, C-313]. A second attempt happens only when the challenge status is "400" or cannot be read [C-302, C-301]. So losing the status (P-2) opens neither a downgrade nor a leak by this path. What it can cause: a provider that does not refresh behind `invalidateToken()` returns the same, already rejected token on the second call and the library sends it a second time; the adapter then sees only `AuthenticationFailedException`. An adapter that synthesises status 400 for every such failure would make the engine call the token endpoint on every authentication failure, and one that synthesises -1 would stop the refresh the engine relies on (Section 5, the three load-bearing checks). Condition 7's test covers the "refresh did not happen" case.

### Q6.d What the vendored library and the app do today, against the pin

| Aspect | Vendored library and app today | Upstream at the pin |
|--------|--------------------------------|---------------------|
| Mechanisms in `authenticate()` | `CRAM_MD5` `k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/store/imap/ImapConnection.java:330`, `PLAIN` `:338`, `EXTERNAL` `:349`, `XOAUTH2` `:358-359`; obsolete `AUTOMATIC` and `LOGIN` enum values (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/AuthType.java:25-26`) fall to "Unhandled authentication method" `ImapConnection.java:361-363` | Same four plus OAUTHBEARER inside the XOAUTH2 path; `NONE` replaces the obsolete values [C-313, C-066] |
| Mechanisms this app uses | PLAIN and XOAUTH2 only: `AuthPreferences.getStoreUri()` `app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/preferences/AuthPreferences.java:247-262` (CRAM-MD5 and EXTERNAL are never written by the app) | Unchanged; the app would use `AuthType.PLAIN` and `AuthType.XOAUTH2` |
| How the XOAUTH2 credential reaches the library | The app builds the whole SASL initial client response itself (`AuthPreferences.generateXOAuth2Token()` `:335-344`) and stores it as the "password" of the store URI (`:247-255`); the library only sends `AUTHENTICATE XOAUTH2 <that string>` after checking `AUTH=XOAUTH2` (`ImapConnection.java:368-374`) | The library builds the response from a token it asks the provider for, on each attempt [C-324, C-331]; the store no longer carries a token |
| Capability preconditions | Only `AUTH=XOAUTH2` (`ImapConnection.java:369-371`) | `SASL-IR` is required for the OAuth path, and the choice between `AUTH=OAUTHBEARER` and `AUTH=XOAUTH2` is the server's advertisement [C-313]; a server that advertises both is authenticated with OAUTHBEARER (a behaviour change for such servers; not tested) |
| Failure signalling | A negative response is rethrown as `XOAuth2AuthenticationFailedException` carrying the status parsed from the server's JSON challenge (`ImapConnection.java:399-400`); the engine refreshes the token on status 400 and retries once with a new transport (`BackupWorker.kt:442-446`) | `AuthenticationFailedException` only; the library retries once itself after `invalidateToken()` [C-300]; no status is exposed (P-2, Section 5) |
| Retry and refresh | None in the library | One library-side retry with `getToken` after `invalidateToken()` [C-300] |

### Q6.e Consequence for the later Google OAuth goal

Upstream's `OAuth2TokenProvider` seam would let the app keep acquiring Google tokens itself and hand them to the IMAP library through its own provider, so a later move to current Google sign-in would replace that provider and leave the IMAP library untouched [C-253, C-300]. Upstream's own provider is tied to AppAuth and the legacy Android modules and is not consumable here, so token acquisition and refresh stay app code [C-333, C-334].

### Q6.f Credentials over an unprotected connection (reference)

Added in rework round 1 (security review M-1). Authentication in this app depends on one more thing that is neither a mechanism nor part of the token seam: whether the connection is protected before the credentials are sent. The library does not decide that. It sends a password on whatever connection the settings describe, including `ConnectionSecurity.NONE`, and this app's settings screen offers "None". The mapping from the stored setting to the connection-security value is code that the app would have to own after a migration. It is set out, with its risk, in Section 7 Q7.h and carried as Section 12 condition 17.

## 7. Custom trust, pinning and SNI

Scope and method. Every upstream result below is **code reading** at the pin (experiment (c) is not run; no proof of concept was compiled). Two recorded runs support it: the existing SNI unit tests were run in the I0 gate (JUnit XML of that run: `DefaultTrustedSocketFactorySniTest` tests=10, skipped=0, failures=0), and a small program in scratch (`<scratch>/q7probe/SniProbe.java`) constructed `javax.net.ssl.SNIHostName` on the build JDK (experiment (g)). Status values are the plan's: **supported** = achievable with app-owned code (including code moved out of `:k9mail-vendored`) through a public interface at the pin; **needs fork of <files>** = a file at the pin must be changed or copied; **blocker** = no path. The central finding is that the mail modules verify **nothing** about TLS themselves: a search of both mail modules for `HostnameVerifier`, `endpointIdentification`, `SSLParameters` and `serverNames` finds only `TrustManagerFactory.java:20` and `:75` [C-240, C-413]; the library creates its socket by calling the supplied `TrustedSocketFactory` at `RealImapConnection.kt` for implicit TLS and again after `STARTTLS` [C-355, C-356, C-067], so hostname verification, pinning and SNI are all decided inside whichever factory the caller passes.

### Q7.a The four protections

| Protection | Extension point | Code reference or proof of concept | Status |
|------------|-----------------|------------------------------------|--------|
| TLS hostname verification | The `X509TrustManager` that the supplied `TrustedSocketFactory` installs in its `SSLContext` [C-067]. Upstream's own trust manager is also reachable: `TrustManagerFactory.createInstance(LocalKeyStore)` and `getTrustManagerForDomain(host, port)` are public in `:mail:common` [C-336, C-337] | Today: vendored `SecureX509TrustManager.checkServerTrusted` runs system validation and then `verifyHostname` (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/ssl/TrustManagerFactory.java:68-78`), which calls `HttpsURLConnection.getDefaultHostnameVerifier()` through the `MinimalSslSession` shim (`:142-150`, `:157-190`). At the pin: the same sequence, but with httpclient5 `DefaultHostnameVerifier.verify(host, certificate)` after a certificate-blocklist check [C-338, C-240, C-344]. The pinned factory does not verify the host name at all; the fingerprint replaces it (`PinnedCertificateSocketFactory.java:126-150`) | **supported**: achievable with a hostname-verifying trust manager as app-owned code moved from `:k9mail-vendored` (`TrustManagerFactory.java` with its shim, used by `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java`), or with upstream's public `TrustManagerFactory` (which adds httpclient5 and a blocklist, and changes the hostname-matching implementation: the vendored code asks the platform's default hostname verifier, upstream's asks httpclient5's `DefaultHostnameVerifier` [C-240, C-338]; whether the two accept the same certificates, for example for wildcards or a common-name fallback, was not tested). No upstream file changes |
| Certificate pinning (`app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail/PinnedCertificateSocketFactory.java`) | The same interface, supplied through the store factory's third parameter `trustedSocketFactory` [C-245, C-067] | `PinnedCertificateSocketFactory implements TrustedSocketFactory` (`:55`) with the four-argument `createSocket` (`:75-76`) that builds an `SSLContext` around a trust manager accepting only the enrolled leaf (SHA-256 equality, then `checkValidity()`, `:126-150`) and returns the socket (`:77-86`); it is chosen in `K9MailTransport.java:135-148`. At the pin the interface has the same FQN and the same erased `(Socket, String, int, String)` shape; only the `MessagingException` named in `throws` is a different class [C-067, C-304]. **Store semantics:** the library's own store (`LocalKeyStore`) has *user-accepted exception* semantics (a certificate stored for host and port is consulted only after system validation fails) [C-340, C-338], whereas the app's pin has *reject everything else* semantics (details in Q7.b). **CNTR-MODERNIZATION-001 measured** (Q7.b): same interface FQN; the `MessagingException` in the signature, the slot form (a factory parameter instead of a super-constructor) and the FQN of producer 1 differ (P-3, P-4, P-5); both producers can still be supplied | **supported**: the file stays app-owned; its only change is the `MessagingException` import. No upstream file changes |
| SNI with the IP-literal and blank-hostname guard | The same interface: the factory sets `SSLParameters.serverNames` on the socket it returns, before the library connects or handshakes [C-355, C-356] | Today: `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.setSniViaSSLParameters` skips SNI when `isIpOrBlankHostname` is true (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/ssl/DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java:216-243`) and catches `IllegalArgumentException` from `SNIHostName` (`:246-253`). At the pin the default factory is in the Android module `:legacy:core` and guards only with `isLegalIPAddress(host) == null`; it has no blank-host guard and no `try`/`catch` around `SNIHostName` [C-345, C-348, C-349]. The ten cases are in Q7.c; result per case: DNS name (3 tests) same as vendored; IPv4 literal same; abbreviated IPv6 literal same; full IPv6 literal: no test exercises it (the test of that name passes the abbreviated `fe80::1`), and an eight-group literal passes the upstream guard by code reading only; empty string and blank host: **differ** (upstream has no guard, so `SNIHostName` throws an unchecked `IllegalArgumentException` instead of the quiet skip); reflective-fallback cases (2 tests): no counterpart upstream (totals: 6 same, 2 no counterpart, 2 differ) | **supported**: achievable with the guarded factory as app-owned code moved from `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java`; upstream's `:legacy:core` factory is not part of the proposed module set |
| Independence from `org.apache.http.legacy` | None needed; this is a dependency property. The only HTTP-library touchpoint in the mail modules is one import, `org.apache.hc` (httpclient5) in `TrustManagerFactory.java` [C-240] | Counts and commands in Q7.d: no mail module, manifest or Gradle file at the pin references `org.apache.http.` or `uses-library`; this repository's compiled sources reference it nowhere | **supported**: nothing to change |

Blockers: **none** (0 of 4 rows). Rows that need a fork of a file at the pin: **none** (0 of 4). Predicate B is therefore false and Section 7 contributes nothing to predicate F. Section 7 does set predicate P (Q7.b). A precondition of all four rows, the mapping from the stored setting to the connection-security value, is recorded in Q7.h; it changes no row status.

### Q7.b Certificate store semantics, and the CNTR-MODERNIZATION-001 measurement

**Store semantics.** Upstream's own certificate store (`LocalKeyStore`) has **user-accepted exception** semantics: a certificate stored for `host:port` is trusted only if it equals the presented one [C-340], it is written by `addCertificate` when a user accepts a certificate [C-341], and the trust manager consults it only after system validation or hostname verification has failed [C-338]. The app's pin has **"reject everything else"** semantics: only the enrolled leaf passes, and any other chain fails with a `CertificateException` (`app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail/PinnedCertificateSocketFactory.java:126-150`). In this app the library store is **inert today**: nothing in `app/src/main` calls `LocalKeyStore.setKeyStoreLocation` (search: `grep -rn setKeyStoreLocation app/src/main k9mail-vendored/src/main` has 3 matches, all inside `LocalKeyStore.java` itself and none outside it), so the vendored `LocalKeyStore` has no key store (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/ssl/LocalKeyStore.java:188-190` throws, the constructor logs and continues `:42-54`) and `isValidCertificate` returns false (`:146-150`). With the default `SYSTEM_VALIDATED` policy the effective rule is therefore system trust plus hostname verification. At the pin the store needs a `KeyStoreDirectoryProvider` [C-339, C-342] whose Android implementation is `internal` in `:legacy:core` [C-343].

**CNTR-MODERNIZATION-001 measured at the pin** (contract file `sdlc/artifacts/design/contracts/CNTR-MODERNIZATION-001-tls-socket-factory.md`; read-only):

| Item | Contract text | At the pin | Difference? |
|------|---------------|------------|-------------|
| Carried type FQN | `com.fsck.k9.mail.ssl.TrustedSocketFactory` (`CNTR-001:84`) | `com.fsck.k9.mail.ssl.TrustedSocketFactory` [C-067] | no |
| `createSocket` signature | `Socket createSocket(Socket socket, String host, int port, String clientCertificateAlias) throws NoSuchAlgorithmException, KeyManagementException, MessagingException, IOException` (`:85-86`); the vendored interface imports `com.fsck.k9.mail.MessagingException` (`k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/ssl/TrustedSocketFactory.java:3,11-12`) | `fun createSocket(socket: Socket?, host: String, port: Int, clientCertificateAlias: String?): Socket` with `@Throws(NoSuchAlgorithmException, KeyManagementException, MessagingException, IOException)`, where `MessagingException` is `net.thunderbird.core.common.exception.MessagingException` [C-067, C-304]. Erased Java parameter and return types are identical | **yes**: the `MessagingException` in the `throws` clause is a different class in a different package; two parameters are now nullable in Kotlin (`socket`, `clientCertificateAlias`) |
| Slot in the store | The k-9 `ImapStore` super-constructor `super(BackupStoreConfig, TrustedSocketFactory, ConnectivityManager)` (`:98-101`, `:276`); breaking-change rule `:284-285` | `ImapStore` is an interface; the factory is the third parameter of the companion function `create(serverSettings, config, trustedSocketFactory, oauthTokenProvider)` [C-245]. The app's `BackupImapStoreDelegate extends ImapStore` (`app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail/transport/K9MailTransport.java:414`) cannot subclass an interface. The carried type is unchanged, so the `:284-285` trigger (a changed type with the slot still expecting the old one) is not met | **yes** in form: constructor super-slot becomes a factory-function parameter |
| Producer 1 (`SYSTEM_VALIDATED`) | `com.fsck.k9.mail.ssl.DefaultTrustedSocketFactory`, built as `new DefaultTrustedSocketFactory(context)` (`:64`, `:95`, `:275`) | The default factory is `com.fsck.k9.helper.DefaultTrustedSocketFactory(context: Context?, trustManagerFactory: TrustManagerFactory)` in `:legacy:core` [C-345], wired by Koin there [C-351]; that module is not in the proposed set | **yes**: package, module and constructor differ; the app-owned moved copy is the producer |
| Producer 2 (`PINNED_CERTIFICATE`) | `com.zegoggles.smssync.mail.PinnedCertificateSocketFactory` (`:96`) | App-owned, unchanged apart from the import | no |

### Q7.c The SNI cases (`DefaultTrustedSocketFactorySniTest`, 10 `@Test` methods)

All ten tests call the vendored `com.fsck.k9.mail.ssl.DefaultTrustedSocketFactory` directly (import `app/src/test/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail/ssl/DefaultTrustedSocketFactorySniTest.java:14`). Upstream's `setSniHost` is `private` inside the companion object of a class in `:legacy:core` [C-348], so none of the ten tests could target it, and the last column is "yes" for every case: the tests exercise, and the app would keep using, the app-owned moved factory. Line numbers are those of the method declarations (the `@Test` annotations are one line earlier). The vendored column is from the test assertions (run in the I0 gate) and the cited vendored lines; the upstream column is code reading of the cited lines, with the `SNIHostName` behaviour taken from experiment (g) (JDK 25.0.5; Android's runtime copy of the class was not run). Upstream decides on `isLegalIPAddress(host) == null` [C-345]: for a name that is not an IPv4 or IPv6 literal the call returns `null`, and SNI is set by `sslParameters.serverNames = listOf(SNIHostName(hostname))` [C-348].

| Test method (line) | Input | Vendored result | Upstream result at pin | Basis | App-owned factory still the one in use? |
|--------------------|-------|-----------------|------------------------|-------|------------------------------------------|
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_setsSSLParametersWithCorrectSNIHostName` (`:48`) | `imap.gmail.com` | `setSSLParameters` called once; one `SNIHostName` equal to the host | SNI set to the host: IPv4 test needs four dot parts (3 found), IPv6 test needs at least 3 colon-separated parts (1 found), so `isLegalIPAddress` is `null` [C-349, C-350] | test run (vendored); code reading (upstream) | yes |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_setsCorrectHostname_forDifferentHost` (`:70`) | `mail.example.org` | SNI equals `mail.example.org` | same as the row above | test run; code reading | yes |
| `reflectiveFallback_withAbsentSetHostnameMethod_doesNotThrow` (`:98`) | fake socket without `setHostname`, host `imap.gmail.com`, private `setHostnameViaReflection` called by reflection | no exception; the catch logs at debug (`DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java:256-266`) | no counterpart: upstream has no reflective path; `SSLCertificateSocketFactory.setHostname` is used only when the factory is an `SSLCertificateSocketFactory`, which a factory from `SSLContext` is not [C-348] | test run; code reading | yes |
| `reflectiveFallback_withAbsentSetHostnameMethod_doesNotCallSetSSLParameters` (`:133`) | mock socket, host `imap.gmail.com`, same private method | no exception; `setSSLParameters` never called | no counterpart (as above) | test run; code reading | yes |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_dnsHostname_setsSNI` (`:151`) | `imap.example.com` | SNI equals the host | SNI set to the host (as row 1) | test run; code reading | yes |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_ipv4Literal_doesNotThrowAndSkipsSNI` (`:171`) | `192.168.1.10` | `isIpOrBlankHostname` true through the IPv4 pattern (`:225`); `setSSLParameters` never called | `isLegalIPv4Address`: four components, each either `0` or one to three digits without a leading zero and at most 255, first not 0, so the literal is returned and SNI is skipped [C-349] | test run; code reading | yes |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_ipv6Literal_doesNotThrowAndSkipsSNI` (`:187`) | `::1` (abbreviated) | true through the colon test (`:221`); skipped | `isLegalIPv6Address`: split on `:` gives `["", "", "1"]` (3 parts); the empty part is expanded to 8 components, none all-zero, so a canonical address is returned and SNI is skipped [C-350] | test run; code reading | yes |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_ipv6FullLiteral_doesNotThrowAndSkipsSNI` (`:202`) | `fe80::1` (the test is named "full" but its input is abbreviated; no eight-group literal is tested) | true through the colon test; skipped | same path as `::1`: parts `["fe80", "", "1"]`, expanded to 8, valid, SNI skipped [C-350]. A true eight-group literal also passes (no empty part, 8 components); by code reading only, not a test | test run; code reading | yes |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_emptyHostname_doesNotThrowAndSkipsSNI` (`:216`) | `` (empty) | `trim().isEmpty()` true (`:217`); skipped, no exception | **Differs.** `"".split(".")` and `"".split(":")` give one part, so `isLegalIPAddress("")` is `null` and `SNIHostName("")` is attempted with no `try`/`catch` [C-349, C-350, C-348]. On JDK 25 the constructor throws `IllegalArgumentException("Server name value of host_name cannot be empty")` (experiment (g)); the exception leaves `createSocket` unchecked | test run; code reading plus experiment (g) | yes (and necessary: this guard exists only in the app-owned factory) |
| `setSniViaSSLParameters_blankHostname_doesNotThrowAndSkipsSNI` (`:231`) | three spaces | `trim().isEmpty()` true; skipped | **Differs.** One part again, `isLegalIPAddress` is `null`, `SNIHostName("   ")` is attempted; on JDK 25 it throws `IllegalArgumentException("Contains non-LDH ASCII characters")` (experiment (g)) and leaves `createSocket` unchecked | test run; code reading plus experiment (g) | yes (necessary, as above) |

Summary: upstream's default factory gives the same result as the vendored one for 6 of the 10 cases (the DNS names and the three literals, which the guard recognises), has no counterpart for 2 (the reflective tests), and differs for 2 (empty and blank host: no guard, so an unchecked `IllegalArgumentException` instead of a quiet skip). If the guarded factory stays app-owned, which is the case Q7.a evaluates, none of the ten is lost: all ten move with the factory, and only their import changes to the new package (a rename).
