The TLS and socket classes each mode would bring in were searched at the pin with a pattern file kept in scratch (`<scratch>/q7pat.re`: `SNIHostName`, `serverNames`, `ApplicationProtocol`, `java.util.function`, `java.util.stream`, `java.time`, `java.nio.file`, `Optional`, `java.util.Base64`, `computeIfAbsent`, `getOrDefault`, `removeIf`, `Duration`, `Files.`, `StandardCharsets`, `Build.VERSION`, `SDK_INT`, `@RequiresApi`, `@TargetApi`, `.stream(`, `CompletableFuture`, `java.util.concurrent`, `List.of`, `Map.of`, `Set.of`), over: `mail/common/.../ssl/`, `CertificateChainException.kt`, `CertificateValidationException.kt`, `core/common/.../net/` (host-name utilities and the blocklist), `RealImapConnection.kt`, and, outside the proposed set, `legacy/core/.../helper/DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.kt` and `KeyChainKeyManager.java`.

This repository's own code ties both to API 24 (Android N): `k9mail-vendored/src/main/java/com/fsck/k9/mail/ssl/DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java:191-196` and `:229-231` guard them with `SDK_INT >= N` |

Result: M1 and M3 do **not** both need an API above 23 (neither does, for the classes either mode brings in; the only API 24 use in the TLS path sits in a class that neither mode needs). **Section 4's minSdk verdict is not reopened** (conflict only in mode M2, non-gating). A dated note recording this check was added at the end of Section 4. Limits: this is a text search, not a bytecode API scan; `:legacy:core`'s Android module floor is minSdk 24 by its convention plugin [C-352, C-353].

**What would have to move into app-owned code if `:k9mail-vendored` were deleted.** At minimum the `SYSTEM_VALIDATED` producer: a `TrustedSocketFactory` implementation with the hardening and the guarded SNI code (`DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java`, 267 lines, and its ten tests), and a trust manager that does system validation plus hostname verification, either the vendored `TrustManagerFactory.java` (191 lines, plus the `CertificateChainException` type that the connection's SSL error handler expects [C-308]) or upstream's public one from `:mail:common`. `PinnedCertificateSocketFactory` is already app-owned. `LocalKeyStore.java` (198 lines) and `KeyChainKeyManager.java` (215 lines) would move only if the user-accepted-exception store and client-certificate authentication are kept; both are inert in this app today (Q7.b; no alias is ever set). Upstream's default factory is not an alternative to this move, because it sits in `:legacy:core`, an Android module whose own dependencies include most of the legacy tree [C-352]. **Also app-owned after a migration (added in rework round 1):** the store-URI mapping that decides TLS, STARTTLS or none, 213 lines of vendored code (`ImapStoreUriDecoder.java` 133, `ImapStoreSettings.java` 52, `helper/UrlEncodingHelper.java` 28), or the 144-line upstream migration class that is outside the proposed modules; see Q7.h. These lines are in addition to the 458 above.

### Q7.g Notes

- **Correction to an earlier statement (dated 2026-11-13).** Q4.c (Section 4) says Android lint does not check plain JVM modules. Both mail modules apply the standalone Android lint plugin [C-362, C-004, C-361]. The minSdk that lint assumes for them was not examined, so the text search of Q4.c remains the API-level evidence. A dated note was added to Section 4; its text was not rewritten.
- **Pinned factory and SNI.** `PinnedCertificateSocketFactory.createSocket` (`:75-86`) neither hardens the socket nor sets server names itself; this is unchanged by the migration, and whether the platform adds SNI to a layered socket created that way was not tested here.
- **Behaviour added upstream.** The upstream trust manager rejects chains containing a listed public-key hash before it calls system validation [C-338, C-344]; the vendored one has no equivalent. An app-owned copy of the vendored trust manager would not have it unless it is copied or re-implemented (a Part B scoping item, not a blocker).

### Q7.h Credentials over an unprotected connection (store-URI mapping and the "no security" choice)

Added in rework round 1 (security review M-1). This sub-section is not a fifth protection row: the four rows of Q7.a and their statuses are unchanged. It records a precondition of all four.

**What the app does today.** (1) The settings screen offers three choices for the connection: TLS (`+ssl+`, the default), STARTTLS (`+tls+`) and None (an empty value) (`app/src/main/res/xml/preferences.xml:46-52`, `app/src/main/res/values/arrays.xml:108-122`, `app/src/main/res/values/strings.xml:262-264`). (2) `AuthPreferences.getStoreUri()` builds `imap<value>://<AUTH>:<user>:<secret>@<host>` from that value (`AuthPreferences.java:247-262,281-290`), so "None" gives the bare scheme `imap://` and an absent preference gives `imap+ssl+://` (`:81,273-275`); the XOAUTH2 path always uses `+ssl+` (`:247-255`). (3) The app's own validators accept all three schemes (`K9MailTransport.java:337-339`, `BackupImapStore.java:236-238`) and no app code refuses a cleartext store. (4) The vendored `ImapStoreUriDecoder` (133 lines) turns the scheme into the library's `ConnectionSecurity`: `imap` to `NONE`, `imap+tls...` to `STARTTLS_REQUIRED`, `imap+ssl...` to `SSL_TLS_REQUIRED` (the obsolete `imap+tls` and `imap+ssl` forms are matched with `startsWith`), and any other scheme to an exception that `ImapStore` reports as "Error while decoding store URI", so an unknown scheme fails closed today (`ImapStoreUriDecoder.java:49-73`, `ImapStore.java:79-84`). The vendored `ImapStore` also refuses a missing user name or password before any network I/O (`ImapStore.java:92-102`, fork commit [C-450]). No test checks any of this mapping (Q7.i).

**What the pin offers.** The mail modules contain no such decoder. The only one is `ImapStoreUriDecoder` in a settings-migration package of `:legacy:storage` (144 lines; the same three branches and the same exception; used once, to convert stored account URIs to the new server-settings format) [C-443, C-444, C-445]. `:legacy:storage` is an Android library with `:legacy:core` among its dependencies [C-446] and is not in the proposed module set (Section 2). The library API takes a ready `ServerSettings` with a `ConnectionSecurity` field [C-249, C-250, C-245]. So after a migration **the app writes, or copies, the mapping itself**. A copy of the migration class is possible (it needs `UrlEncodingHelper`, which is in `:mail:common` [C-447]); it is not a drop-in: it defaults the authentication type to PLAIN at declaration and returns the new `ServerSettings`, and it carries the empty-last-field fix of fork commit [C-451] [C-444].

**What the library does with the value (code reading at the pin).** The connection reads `connectionSecurity` in exactly two places: implicit TLS asks the supplied factory for the socket and anything else opens a bare `Socket()` [C-355]; STARTTLS requires the server to advertise the capability (otherwise `MissingCapabilityException`; the vendored library throws `CertificateValidationException` instead, `ImapConnection.java:293-302`) and then hands the socket to the factory [C-441, C-356]. Nothing else consults it before authentication [C-440, C-313]. With `NONE` and a password type the library authenticates: SASL PLAIN if the server advertises `AUTH=PLAIN`, otherwise the `LOGIN` command unless the server advertises `LOGINDISABLED` [C-314, C-327]; `LOGINDISABLED` is not consulted on the SASL path or on the `LOGIN` fallback after a failed SASL PLAIN [C-315]. **The library never refuses to send a password on an unprotected connection.** The vendored library behaves the same (`ImapConnection.java:336-347`). With `NONE` the supplied factory is never called, so the hostname, pinning and SNI protections of Q7.a do not apply to that connection at all.

**The risk, stated plainly.** The library's behaviour is unchanged by a migration. What changes is who writes the line that decides TLS, STARTTLS or none. A wrong or missing value there (a default of `NONE`, a stored value that falls through to `NONE`, an `imap+tls` mapped to `NONE`) would send the user name and password in the clear (the XOAUTH2 path always uses `+ssl+` today) while every row of Q7.a still reads "supported" and every SNI and pinning test still passes, because none of them runs on that connection. It is the one place where a migration could weaken credential protection without touching any of the four rows. Today the missing-value cases fail closed: an unknown scheme throws, an absent preference gives TLS, missing credentials are refused. Section 12 condition 17 carries that property over by test.

**App-owned code that this adds (line counts).** `ImapStoreUriDecoder.java` 133 + `ImapStoreSettings.java` 52 + `UrlEncodingHelper.java` 28 = **213 lines** of vendored code whose function the app would own, or the 144-line upstream migration class plus `UrlEncodingHelper` [C-443, C-447]. How much is written depends on whether the app keeps a URI at all, which is a Part B design choice not made here.

**What this does not decide.** Whether the app should keep offering "None" is a product question about a feature that exists today. This analysis does not propose removing it and does not call it acceptable: it records that the choice exists and that the library does not guard it.

### Q7.i What the current tests prove about the four protections

Added in rework round 1 (security review M-2). Facts only.

- The search `grep -rn -i -E "hostname.?(mismatch|verif)|wrong.?host|HostnameVerifier|TrustManagerFactory|SecureX509|CertificateValidationException|CertificateChainException" app/src/test` returns **0 lines**. `k9mail-vendored/src/test` does not exist (`git ls-files k9mail-vendored` lists no `/src/test/` path). **No hostname-mismatch test exists today.**
- `DefaultTrustedSocketFactorySniTest` calls the static `setSniViaSSLParameters(mockSocket, host)` on a Mockito `SSLSocket` (`app/src/test/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail/ssl/DefaultTrustedSocketFactorySniTest.java:48-66`) and reaches one private method by reflection (`:253-255`): no connection and no handshake, so the ten cases pass on code that a migration copies unchanged (Q7.c).
- `PinnedCertificateSocketFactoryTest` calls `checkServerTrusted` on the trust manager obtained through the package-private `getTrustManagerForTesting()` (`PinnedCertificateSocketFactory.java:94`; test `:218`). Its mismatch case presents a different certificate (`:107-112`), not a certificate for a different host, and no handshake takes place.
- The only test that runs against a live server, `BackupImapStoreDelegateIntegrationTest` (GreenMail), connects with `imap://PLAIN:...@127.0.0.1:<port>` (`:85-89,144`), the unprotected path: no test ever drives a TLS or STARTTLS connection through the store, and none tests the scheme-to-security mapping of Q7.h.
- Consequence: no test today fails if a certificate for the wrong host is accepted, and none would show that a new library routes both connection paths through the app's factory. This is a gap in the current app's tests regardless of the migration decision; it is listed in Section 12 Q12.f items 8 and 9. REQ-MODERNIZATION-019 AC-B8 already asks for a hostname-mismatch test and a pinned accept / different reject test (`REQ-MODERNIZATION-019:109`); condition 12 states how they are shown to prove the migration.

## 8. Size and method count

**Upstream was not built, and this app was not compiled against it.** Experiments (c) (build the upstream mail modules) and (d) (compile a throwaway copy of this app against them) were deliberately not run, per plan decision D2: no acceptance criterion requires a build (AC-7 accepts code citations for "yes" and "via shim", and AC-10 accepts an estimate labelled as such); a build would run upstream's own untrusted build logic with a newer Gradle, AGP and JDK floor than this repository and about 1-2 GB of downloads; and it cannot change the type of the recommendation, which turns on capability gaps (Sections 5 and 7), licences (Section 3) and platform conflicts (Section 4), all of which come from reading. The baseline below is **measured** on this repository; every number about the upstream side is an **estimate** with its method and its error sources stated. The residual uncertainty is carried to Sections 9 and 11 as a cost range and as a numbered condition ("measure on `assembleRelease` in the first Part B story"). This departs from the story's Estimation Guidance, which expected small build experiments; the product owner is told so at presentation.

### Q8.a Baseline at the start commit (measured)

| Measure | Baseline | Tool and command |
|---------|----------|------------------|
| Release APK size | **1,995,017 bytes** (`app-release-unsigned.apk`; unsigned because no keystore properties exist on this machine) | `./gradlew ... :app:assembleRelease` (gate run in the Baseline section), then `wc -c < app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release*.apk` |
| `classes.dex` in the APK | single dex; 2,533,448 bytes, 1,072,957 bytes deflated in the APK | `unzip -lv app/build/outputs/apk/release/app-release-unsigned.apk` |
| Dex method count | **23,904** (`method_ids_size`) | `<sdk>/build-tools/36.0.0/dexdump -f classes.dex` (`apkanalyzer` was not used: it failed on this machine in sprint-018) |
| Dex class count | **3,627** (`class_defs_size`) | the same `dexdump -f` |
| Defined methods and code bytes (derived) | 20,167 defined methods; 1,055,670 bytes of code (sum of `insns size` x 2) | `dexdump -d classes.dex`, parsed in scratch; the class total equals `class_defs_size` (3,627) |

The dex holds 23,904 method ids against 20,167 defined methods: the difference is methods that are only referenced (framework and library methods). R8 (version 9.0.32, min API 21) was in use; one dex file is enough (23,904 is far below the 65,536 limit).

### Q8.b What the current mail stack contributes (measured)

The stack is the vendored module and its four libraries. Attribution: every class in the release dex was mapped back to its original name through the R8 `mapping.txt` of the same build and grouped by original package prefix. Checks: the grouped class total equals `class_defs_size`; the per-prefix class counts equal the counts in `mapping.txt` (120, 156, 8, 8, 7); method ids rebuilt from definitions plus `// method@` references give 23,901 against the header's 23,904 (3 method ids are not matched by the parser). "Original" sizes are counted from the class files of the original artifacts (bytes parsed; nothing executed): the vendored module's own javac output in this repository's `k9mail-vendored/build/` (build output, not tracked) and the library jars at the vendored versions [C-409, C-410, C-411].

| Component | Original classes | Original methods | Retained classes | Retained defined methods | Retained code bytes | Class retention | Method retention | Jar bytes |
|-----------|------------------|------------------|------------------|--------------------------|---------------------|-----------------|------------------|-----------|
| `:k9mail-vendored` own code (`com.fsck.k9`) | 170 | 1,308 | 120 | 738 | 66,370 | 70.6% | 56.4% | 611,919 (module output) |
| mime4j core + dom 0.8.11 | 272 | 2,651 | 156 | 711 | 43,506 | 57.4% | 26.8% | 469,869 |
| commons-io 2.16.1 | 346 | 3,235 | 8 | 51 | 1,160 | 2.3% | 1.6% | 508,826 |
| jzlib 1.0.7 | 14 | 130 | 8 | 68 | 12,020 | 57.1% | 52.3% | 48,521 |
| jutf7 1.0.0 | 7 | 42 | 7 | 40 | 2,102 | 100% | 95.2% | 12,333 |
| **Mail stack total** | 809 | 7,366 | **299** | **1,608** | **125,158** | 37.0% | 21.8% | 1,651,468 |

Result for deleting the stack (all three consumption modes remove it): **-299 classes** (8.2% of 3,627), **-1,608 defined methods** (8.0% of 20,167), **-1,810 method ids** (7.6% of 23,904: the 1,608 definitions plus 202 external methods referenced only by this stack), and about **85,551 to 127,208 bytes** of the APK (4.3% to 6.4% of 1,995,017): the low figure is the stack's share of defined methods (1,608 of 20,167) times the 1,072,957 deflated dex bytes, the high figure is its share of code bytes (125,158 of 1,055,670) times the same. The two readings are simple apportionments of the deflated dex, not a direct measurement of the bytes the stack occupies: the dex also holds strings, types and debug data that neither apportions. The four third-party libraries alone account for 870 defined methods and 58,788 code bytes (46,287 to 59,751 APK bytes).

### Q8.c Estimation method (fixed by the plan) and its inputs

Both deltas are **estimates**. Notation: M1 = JVM variants of the upstream modules built from source; M2 = Android variants; M3 = source inclusion (Section 4).

1. **Bytes.** `delta_bytes = k_b x (1) + (3)`. (1) = bytes of runtime jars added or changed by the mode, minus the jar bytes they replace (the jar table below; jar bytes are file sizes, which equal the registry `Content-Length`). `k_b` = the current stack's retained APK bytes per jar byte at baseline, taken for the four third-party libraries: low 46,287 / 1,039,549 = **0.0445**, high 59,751 / 1,039,549 = **0.0575**. (3) = the source-module term: upstream source set size set against the vendored module's, times the vendored module's own measured retained footprint `F` (738 defined methods: 39,264 bytes by method share; 66,370 code bytes: 67,457 bytes by code share): `(3) = (upstream / vendored - 1) x F`, low with the line-count ratio and `F` low, high with the top-level-declaration ratio and `F` high.
2. **Methods.** `delta_methods = m x (retained-class delta)`, with `m` = retained defined methods per retained class of the current stack = 1,608 / 299 = **5.378**, converted to method ids with the id-per-definition factor **1.1256** (this stack) to **1.1853** (the whole dex). Retained classes added: for each new jar, its class entries times a class retention ratio observed at baseline (low: commons-io, 8 / 346 = 2.3%; high: the four third-party libraries together, 179 / 639 = 28.0%); for source modules, top-level declarations times the expansion factor observed for the vendored module (170 compiled classes / 102 declarations = **1.667**) times a class retention ratio (low 28.0%, the third-party aggregate; high 70.6%, the vendored module itself). The declaration count is the upstream count for the high end (M1 262, M3 134) and, for the low end, the vendored module's 102 declarations scaled by the line-count ratio (M1 102 x 1.107, M3 102 x 0.765). Worked out: source term 52.7 to 308.2 retained classes (M1) and 36.4 to 157.6 (M3); new jars 2,909 (M1) or 2,081 (M3) classes x 2.3% to 28.0% = 67.3 to 814.9 and 48.1 to 582.9; the version-change jars add 3.3 (mime4j +5 classes at 28.0%, commons-io +82 at 2.3%); sum 123.3 to 1,126.4 (M1) and 87.8 to 743.9 (M3), the figures of Q8.d. Removed: the measured 299 classes and 1,810 method ids.
3. **Counting declarations.** Top-level declarations are counted with the plan's regex, with the modifier group extended per plan-review note R3 to `(final |abstract |open |data |sealed |static |annotation |value |inline |expect |actual )*` (pattern file `<scratch>/q8/classre.re`; command `git grep -c -E -f <scratch>/q8/classre.re ac1f0e85... -- <source sets>`). The regex counts only declarations that start in column 0 (nested and anonymous classes are not counted; the expansion factor above absorbs them). **Sample check on 5 files** (one a Kotlin `commonMain` file): `core/common/.../net/Domain.kt` 1 (`value class`) [C-363]; `ImapFolder.kt` 2 (two interfaces) [C-364]; `MimeMessage.java` 1 (the nested class at line 487 is not counted by design) [C-365]; `AuthType.kt` 1 (`enum class`) [C-066]; `HostNameUtils.kt` 1 (`object`) [C-366]. Each equals the hand count of top-level declarations. Result sets: upstream M1 sets (the two mail modules plus the six KMP modules' `commonMain`, `commonJvmMain` and `jvmMain`): 249 files, 19,656 lines, 262 declarations [C-374, C-375, C-376, C-377, C-378, C-379, C-380, C-381]; the vendored module (webdav excluded, `find ... -name "*.java"`): 102 files, 17,759 lines, 102 declarations.
4. **M3 source set.** Computed by an import-closure heuristic (script in scratch; no compile): start from the upstream types the app would use (the replacement types of Section 2, the IMAP store, folder, message, connection classes, the token provider, the trust classes) and add every file whose explicitly imported type, wildcard-imported package name or same-package name is referenced. Result: 128 files, 13,581 lines, 134 declarations. It over-approximates inside each package (same-package name matches) and cannot see Kotlin implicit references; it is a stand-in, not a measurement.
5. **What is not counted.** `kotlin-stdlib`, `kotlinx-coroutines-core`, `androidx.annotation` and JetBrains `annotations` are already in the app at other versions; their version changes belong to REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 and -016, not to this migration, and are excluded. `slf4j-api` and the serialization libraries are counted as new: `app/build.gradle` and `k9mail-vendored/build.gradle` do not declare them (the metadata file records slf4j-api 1.7.36 and serialization 1.6.3, whose role in the release runtime classpath was not resolved here).
6. **Library sets per mode.** M1 and M2 count every jar the Gradle resolution of experiment (f) brings; M3 counts only the libraries that a file of the closure imports (`okio` [C-370, C-371]; Moshi [C-335]; httpclient5 [C-240]; the Thunderbird logging and outcome components [C-372]; `kotlinx.serialization` [C-369]) and leaves out Koin (imported only by two `:core:common` files [C-367, C-368] that the closure does not reach), stately, `kotlinx-datetime` and `kotlinx-collections-immutable` (no file of the M1 source sets imports the last two; `git grep` over the sets returns 0 files for each of the three).

### Q8.d Results (estimates)

| Measure | Baseline at start commit | Delta | Measured or estimate | Method and tool |
|---------|--------------------------|-------|----------------------|-----------------|
| APK bytes, removal of the mail stack (all modes) | 1,995,017 | -85,551 to -127,208 | measured attribution (assumes the whole stack is deleted) | `dexdump -d` joined with `mapping.txt`, Q8.b |
| **APK bytes, M1 JVM variants** | 1,995,017 | **about +237,000 to +407,000** (APK about 2.23 to 2.40 MB, +11.9% to +20.4%) | **estimate** | plan formula `k_b x (1) + (3)`: term (1) 5,237,177 bytes x k_b 0.0445 to 0.0575, term (3) +4,200 to +105,800 |
| APK bytes, M1, cross-check | 1,995,017 | about -92,000 to +394,000 | estimate | retained classes x m x bytes per defined method (53.2 to 79.1) minus the removal |
| **APK bytes, M2 Android variants** | 1,995,017 | same as M1; the Android-only libraries (core-ktx, koin-android, coroutines-android and their AndroidX closure) are not imported by any mail source file, so none is retained by the model; version changes they force on AndroidX libraries the app already has were **not measured** (needs the Android resolution that only a build performs; no attempt made, experiments (c) and (d) are not run) | **estimate** | as M1 |
| **APK bytes, M3 source inclusion** | 1,995,017 | **about +158,000 to +237,000** (APK about 2.15 to 2.23 MB, +7.9% to +11.9%) | **estimate** | plan formula with the M3 source set and libraries: (1) 3,750,428 bytes, term (3) -9,200 to +21,200 |
| APK bytes, M3, cross-check | 1,995,017 | about -102,000 to +231,000 | estimate | as the M1 cross-check |
| **Dex method count (method ids), M1 and M2** | 23,904 | **about -1,060 to +5,370** (23,904 becomes about 22,840 to 29,270) | **estimate** | `m x retained-class delta` x id factor, minus 1,810 |
| **Dex method count (method ids), M3** | 23,904 | **about -1,280 to +2,930** (becomes about 22,630 to 26,840) | **estimate** | as above with the M3 sets |
| Dex class count, M1 and M2 | 3,627 | about -180 to +830 | estimate | added retained classes 123 to 1,126 minus 299 |
| Dex class count, M3 | 3,627 | about -210 to +440 | estimate | added retained classes 88 to 744 minus 299 |

Reading the results. The byte formula gives a **net increase** in every mode (the plan formula's low end is already positive, because it charges the new libraries at the baseline retained share of jar bytes whether or not any mail source references them); the cross-check, which retains only a share of classes, gives a range that reaches below zero in M1 and M3, so a small net decrease cannot be excluded. The dex stays far below the 65,536 method limit in every case. The wide ranges are the honest consequence of not building: R8 retained 1.6% of commons-io's methods and 95.2% of jutf7's, so the retention applied to a library that has not been compiled against can move the answer by an order of magnitude.

**Error sources (what could make the real figures differ).** (a) R8 retention: the ratios come from five baseline stacks and span 1.6% to 95.2% of methods; upstream's Kotlin code and its libraries were never shrunk by R8 here. (b) Libraries the mail sources do not reference (Koin beyond two files, `kotlinx-datetime`, collections-immutable, stately) would be removed by R8 in practice; the plan formula still charges them in M1 and M2, which makes M1's byte range pessimistic at the low end. (c) Libraries the app already has (Kotlin standard library, coroutines, `androidx.annotation`, JetBrains annotations) are excluded; their version moves belong to REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 and -016. (d) The expansion factor and methods-per-line figures come from Java code (the vendored module); upstream is mostly Kotlin, whose compiled shape differs. (e) Jar bytes are compressed JVM class files; the APK holds compressed dex, so `k_b` is a conversion factor measured on the four baseline libraries, not a law. (f) The M3 closure is a heuristic. (g) Resources, assets, the manifest and baseline profiles are not modelled. (h) A newer R8 or AGP (REQ-MODERNIZATION-015) shifts every number, baseline included.

### Q8.e Verification metadata: new entries a migration would add

Read-only: counted from the metadata files that experiment (f) fetched into its scratch Gradle home and from the 27 jars, against the existing `gradle/verification-metadata.xml` (803 components, 1,460 artifact entries); the writer (`--write-verification-metadata`) was not run (experiment rule 2). Command idiom per coordinate (plan): `grep -c '<component group="G" name="A" version="V"' gradle/verification-metadata.xml` (0 means a new component; example: apache-mime4j-core 0.8.11 returns 1, so 0.8.15 is new).

| Mode | New components | New artifact entries, by artifact | Basis |
|------|----------------|-----------------------------------|-------|
| M1 JVM variants | 54 (42 of the 45 JVM coordinates below plus 12 parent or BOM components) | **112**: 24 `.jar`, 34 `.module`, 54 `.pom` | counted: files fetched by experiment (f) plus its 27 jars |
| M3 source inclusion | 41 | **80**: 18 `.jar`, 21 `.module`, 41 `.pom` | counted: M1 minus the 32 entries of the four families that M3 does not need (koin, stately, `kotlinx-datetime`, `kotlinx-collections-immutable`) |
| M2 Android variants | 54 + 48 Android-only = 102 | **208 to 256** (112 counted + 96 to 144 estimated at 2 to 3 artifacts for each of the 48 Android-only components) | the Android-only part is a POM walk of 72 modules (Section 3, Q3.b), 53 of which are absent at the exact version and 5 of those are already in the M1 set; per-artifact counts for AAR components are estimated, not resolved by Gradle |

Correction found in the I4 consistency pass (2026-11-13): the first version of this table gave 111 (23 jar), 79 (17 jar) and 207 to 255. The recount of the 27 jars of the jar table above against `gradle/verification-metadata.xml` (3 jars already recorded: slf4j-api 1.7.36, jzlib 1.0.7, jutf7 1.0.0; 24 new) found that the jar list used for the first count held 26 jars and omitted `androidx.annotation:annotation-jvm` 1.11.0 (62,221 bytes; its `.module` and `.pom` were counted, its `.jar` was not, and the component has no jar entry in the file at that version). The figures above are the corrected ones; no predicate, row or condition depends on them.

**Overlap with REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 and -016 (added in rework round 1, code review W3).** The counts above are "against the start commit". They include entries for components that the toolchain requirements would add anyway: the families whose version changes Q8.c item 5 assigns to REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 and -016 and excludes from the byte and method estimates (the Kotlin bom and standard library family, `kotlinx-coroutines` bom, core and core-jvm, `androidx.annotation` and `annotation-jvm`, the JetBrains annotations). Condition 1 makes those requirements precede Part B, so when Part B runs those entries are already present. Counted from the same data (`<scratch>/ir1/overlap.py`, which reads `gradle/verification-metadata.xml` and the experiment (f) Gradle home): the overlap is **23 entries on 11 components** in both modes (6 `.jar`, 6 `.module`, 11 `.pom`). Both ways:

| Mode | Counted against the start commit (as above) | Of which on the REQ-MODERNIZATION-015/016 families | Net of those families |
|------|---------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------------|-----------------------|
| M1 JVM variants | 112 (24 jar, 34 module, 54 pom) on 54 components | 23 on 11 components | **89** (18 jar, 28 module, 43 pom) on 43 components |
| M3 source inclusion | 80 (18 jar, 21 module, 41 pom) on 41 components | 23 on 11 components | **57** (12 jar, 15 module, 30 pom) on 30 components |
| M2 Android variants | 208 to 256 | at least the 23 above (the Android-only estimate was not split) | about 185 to 233 |

This is an estimate in one respect: the versions that REQ-MODERNIZATION-015 and -016 will pick are "latest stable at the implementing story's planning date" (Section 4), so the overlapping entries may be for later versions than the 2.4.21 and 1.11.0 used here; the net counts do not depend on that. The size estimates of Q8.d already exclude these families, so the net counts are the ones that match them. The other sections keep quoting the "against the start commit" figures (80, 112, 208 to 256) and now refer to this paragraph for the net ones.

The 12 parent or BOM components that M1 adds beyond the coordinate list are: `io.opentelemetry:opentelemetry-bom:1.49.0`, `org.apache.commons:commons-parent:98`, `org.apache.httpcomponents.client5:httpclient5-parent:5.6.5`, `org.apache.httpcomponents.core5:httpcore5-parent:5.4.4`, `org.apache.httpcomponents:httpcomponents-parent:14`, `org.apache.james:apache-mime4j-project:0.8.15`, `org.apache:apache:27`, `org.apache:apache:35`, `org.apache:apache:37`, `org.jetbrains.kotlin:kotlin-bom:2.4.20`, `org.junit:junit-bom:5.13.3` and `org.junit:junit-bom:5.14.2`. ## 9. Fallback options

Scope. Section 1 found no mail module published as a Maven artifact (five registries, each with a control probe; Q1.c). The option "consume a published artifact" therefore does not exist here: AC-11's "not required" statement applies only when Section 1 finds a published artifact, which it did not, so the fallbacks below are required and are the real routes. Because consumption is source-only, **Options 1 and 2 are the two ways to obtain upstream mail code at all**: Option 1 is the Section 4 mode M3 (source inclusion, upstream files copied into this repository) and Option 2 is mode M1 or M2 (upstream consumed from a fork repository as an included build, a submodule or a self-published artifact). The migration weighed in Section 12 is therefore Option 1 or Option 2; Option 3 is "stay". Costs are counted from Sections 2, 5, 7 and 8 and from this repository; a figure marked "estimate" rests on the unbuilt estimate of Section 8 (upstream was not built, so the residual uncertainty is carried here as ranges and in Section 12 as a numbered condition). In context-map terms (a label, not a cited claim): Option 3 keeps the app conformist to a frozen model; Options 1 and 2 make it conformist to a moving upstream snapshot unless the anti-corruption layer is widened (Section 11).

Not an option (Section 1): consume a published artifact. Result of the five registry probes: none found [C-006 to C-024]. If a mail artifact appears later, this table should be redone with a fourth row.

Routes that keep the module vendored without migrating (an intermediate upstream snapshot, R-A, and selective backport of upstream fixes, R-B) are assessed in Q9.f below, after Q9.e.

**Result: no explicit upstream statement on third-party reuse, API stability or support of the `mail:*` modules was found.** The only stated upstream position is that these modules are legacy, being replaced and to be retired [C-414, C-415]. A maintainer enquiry was not sent (not allowed in Part A); it is a follow-up in Section 12.

### Q9.c Every "needs fork" verdict has non-fork alternatives already listed

Section 5 Q5.b lists them and their cost: for APPEND with the internal date, accept the server-assigned date (a contradiction of `REQ-MODERNIZATION-019:84` and AC-B5 `:106`), or provide the raw command from the app (not possible: the connection type is `internal` [C-274]); for UID SEARCH by header, a `TEXT` search with a client-side filter (one body download per candidate), per-datatype folders (a mailbox migration), or the range search (not a substitute). They are not re-argued here. The F predicate in Section 12 counts the two rows once, as forks. For the read timeout (Q5.t) the non-fork alternatives are accepting 60 s or an app-owned socket wrapper (not tried); the setting is counted once under F as a third fork-dependent item inside row 1.

### Q9.d Upstream cadence (supply-chain evidence, counted with `git log` in the clone at the pin)

`mail/common/src/main`: 252 commits all-time, 17 since 2025-11-13, 6 since 2026-08-13, latest 2026-11-04 [C-427, C-425]. `mail/protocols/imap/src/main`: 233, 16 and 2, latest 2026-09-30 [C-428, C-426]. Whole repository: 918 commits since 2026-08-13 [C-430]. Reading: the modules are still edited (security changes among them: the blocklist of 2026-10-26 [C-423]), with 2 commits in the IMAP module and 6 in the common module over the last three months; the same upstream calls them legacy (Q9.b, 1b). Cadence is evidence about patch flow, not a support commitment.

### Q9.e Residual uncertainty carried as ranges

Because upstream was not built: APK bytes +158,000 to +407,000 (Options 1 and 2, estimate); method ids -1,280 to +5,370; verification entries 80 (Option 1) to 112 (Option 2, M1) or 208 to 256 (M2); the Android-library minimum API of upstream's third-party libraries, whether upstream's build runs on JDK 25 and the plugin-artifact closure are not measured. Section 12 turns these into one numbered condition: the first Part B story builds the chosen mode and measures.

### Q9.f Refreshing the vendored module: an intermediate snapshot (R-A) and selective backport (R-B)

*Added in rework round 2 (2026-11-13).* **This sub-section answers the question recorded as Record 2 in the appendix "Product owner decision": "could we keep it vendored but use the latest version?"** The literal form is Option 1 above (the pinned head copied into this repository, mode M3), which is the migration itself and is already counted in Q9.a. What the earlier sections had not weighed is the middle ground, assessed here:

- **Route R-A, an intermediate snapshot:** re-vendor the mail library at an upstream commit later than the fork's merge base `23bf474d9389fa658b7395cf2637c0ec88d45709` (2016-10-20) [C-483] but earlier than the changes that make the pinned head expensive for this app.
- **Route R-B, selective backport:** keep the current vendored code, port chosen upstream security and robustness fixes into it (IMAP, MIME and TLS paths) and bump its third-party libraries.

This is an assessment only: nothing was built, applied or compiled, no product, build or dependency file changed, and no design, story or acceptance criterion is proposed. It does **not** change the Recommendation of Section 12, which answers REQ-MODERNIZATION-019's question about the Thunderbird libraries at the pin: no predicate value, no row (T2) and no Section 5 or Section 7 status moved. Upstream statements below were read in the clone (blob-less partial clone with full commit and tree history); statements about this repository cite file and line.

**Reading rules.** Dates are author dates, and a rebased commit can be authored long before it landed ("IMAP refactoring" was authored 2017-06-12 and committed 2017-11-09 [C-456]). "Mail paths" are all files under `k9mail-library/` and `mail/`, tests included; "main-source paths" are `src/main` of `k9mail-library`, `mail/common` and `mail/protocols/imap`. Java and Kotlin counts are main-source files of `k9mail-library` and every `mail/**` module except webdav and `mail/testing`, so they include the pop3 and smtp code that the vendored copy also carries (101 Java files at the merge base against the vendored copy's 102 compiled files). The candidates are ancestors of the pin on one line of history, so for each of them the non-merge commits ahead of the merge base and behind the pin add up to the same 732 in the mail paths (a check I ran).

**1. Timeline: where the code lived and what changed for this app**

The library was one Android module, `k9mail-library/`, until 2018-05-23, when it moved to `mail/common/` and its IMAP part to `mail/protocols/imap/` [C-458, C-459]. Each row names the first commit that introduced the change.

| Date | Commit | Change | Effect on this app |
|------|--------|--------|--------------------|
| 2016-10-24 | `7774ebc788` | upstream XOAUTH2: the `ImapStore` constructor gains an `OAuth2TokenProvider` parameter [C-455, C-521, C-529] | the two constructor calls (`BackupImapStore.java:73`, `K9MailTransport.java:420`) gain an argument; the fork's own XOAUTH2 (fork commit [C-033]) and its status-carrying exception no longer have a counterpart upstream |
| 2017-06-12 (landed 2017-11-09) | `aa7e00263f` | "IMAP refactoring": the private `search(ImapSearcher, listener)` method of `ImapFolder` is removed [C-456, C-522, C-535] | the hook that the fork's visibility patch exposes has nothing to expose from here on |
| 2018-03-12 | `130efa2991` | first Kotlin file in the mail library main source (`FolderNotFoundException.kt`) [C-457] | Kotlin enters the module; the library is all Java before this |
| 2018-05-23 | `7397402ba7`, `6961295de4` | module move and split into `:mail:common` and `:mail:protocols:imap` [C-458, C-459] | module shape differs from the single vendored module |
| 2018-07-16 | `f046313d98` | `ImapStore` takes `ImapStoreSettings`; the store-URI decoder leaves the module [C-460, C-544, C-546] | the constructor and `StoreConfig.getStoreUri` path that `BackupStoreConfig` and `BackupImapStore` use end here [C-547] |
| 2018-08-14 | `5c50f367e4` | `AuthenticationFailedException` becomes Kotlin, the first of the 33 names to do so [C-461] | none for Java callers |
| 2020-01-26 | `9335080545`, `58f32df670` | the `RemoteStore` base class and the abstract `Folder` are deleted (`FolderType` had left `Folder` on 2020-01-09 [C-464]) [C-462, C-463] | `Folder`, `Folder.FolderType` and `Folder.OPEN_MODE_RW` stop existing |
| 2020-02-29 | `21891f1d1d` | `MimeHeader` becomes Kotlin [C-465] | none for Java callers |
| 2020-04-18 | `d70564e423` | `ImapSearcher.java` is deleted [C-466] | the app's `new ImapSearcher() {...}` has no type |
| 2020-05-01 | `767f7e0efe` | `StoreConfig` is deleted [C-467] | `BackupStoreConfig` has nothing to implement |
| 2020-06-08 | `dc119f9068` | `ImapFolder` becomes a **final Kotlin class with an internal constructor** [C-468, C-555] | `BackupFolder extends ImapFolder` can no longer compile |
| 2021-06-03 | `55c54555ff`, `94f630cf88` | `ImapFolder`, then `ImapStore`, become interfaces; the implementations are internal; `ImapStore.create(...)` is the factory [C-469, C-470, C-556] | **`ImapStore` is no longer subclassable** |
| 2022-04-07, 2022-04-28 | `a796b1d941`, `7f343e4fd1` | `NetworkType` deleted; `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory` and `KeyChainKeyManager` leave the mail library for the app module [C-471, C-472] | the bundled TLS factory is gone from the library; `TrustManagerFactory`, `LocalKeyStore` and `TrustedSocketFactory` stay |
| 2022-05-02 | `e7032255c6` | both mail modules become plain JVM modules; Moshi and httpclient5 join `:mail:common` [C-473, C-559] | the library no longer has a minSdk of its own |
| 2025-06-04 | `f88679b190` | **first dependency of the mail library on another project module**, `:core:logging:impl-legacy`, a Kotlin Multiplatform module [C-474, C-557, C-558] | KMP modules enter the build |

Between the merge base and 2025-06-04 the two mail build files declare no project dependency other than each other and `:mail:testing` (read at seven commits from `6961295de4` to `dd3d103fd3`; `git log -G` for a project dependency on `core`, `app`, `backend`, `ui`, `feature` or `legacy` in the Groovy build files returns nothing). Main-source Java / Kotlin split (counted from the tree at each commit): merge base 101 / 0 [C-483]; 5.304 116 / 0; 5.501 119 / 0; 2018-05-23 125 / 5; 2018-07-16 128 / 6; 5.700 123 / 14 [C-477, C-478, C-479, C-480, C-481]; pin 56 / 112 [C-001]. Twelve of the classes behind the 33 names are still Java at the pin: `FetchProfile`, `Message`, `MimeMessage`, `MimeMultipart`, `MimeBodyPart`, `MimeUtility`, `MimeMessageHelper`, `TextBody`, `BinaryTempFileBody`, `TrustManagerFactory`, `K9MailLib`, `Base64OutputStream` [C-001]. Build requirements over time: merge base Android library, minSdk 15, Java 1.7, mime4j 0.7.2, commons-io 2.4 [C-483]; the Gradle plugin `kotlin-android` is applied from 5.304 on although no Kotlin main file exists until 2018-03-12 [C-532]; minSdk 21, compileSdk 29, Java 1.8, Kotlin 1.3.50 at 5.700 [C-551]; plain JVM modules from 2022-05-02 [C-559].

**3. Fit to this app**

*(a) API fit.* Method: for each of the 33 distinct `com.fsck.k9.*` names of Section 11 (31 imported names, the nested type `FetchProfile.Item`, and the bare package mention `com.fsck.k9.mail` in a Javadoc line, `K9MailTransport.java:69`, which is not a type), does a file with the same package path and class name exist at the commit (nested names: is the nested type declared in it). Result: **31 of the 32 type names exist at A0, A1, A2, A3 and A4**; the one missing at every candidate is the fork-only `XOAuth2AuthenticationFailedException` [C-576, C-577, C-578]. For comparison the same test gives 27 of 32 at `be7849eb5d` and at `55c54555ff` (missing: `Folder`, `Folder.FolderType`, `StoreConfig`, `ImapSearcher`, and the fork-only type), 25 of 32 at C4 (also missing `NetworkType` and `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory`) and 23 of 32 at the pin. Names existing is not the same as signatures fitting. A line-level comparison of declarations, vendored copy against candidate, gives these differences for the members the adapter calls or overrides (not compiled):

| Member (use in the app) | A0 (5.304) | A1 (5.501) | A2, A3 | A4 (5.700) |
|------------------------|------------|------------|--------|------------|
| `ImapStore` constructor (`BackupImapStore.java:73`, `K9MailTransport.java:420`) | gains an `OAuth2TokenProvider` parameter [C-521] | same [C-572] | same; the URI is still decoded inside from `StoreConfig` [C-544] | takes `ImapStoreSettings` first [C-546]; the app must decode the URI itself |
| `StoreConfig` (`BackupStoreConfig`, 20 declarations) | identical to the vendored interface [C-530] | adds abstract `shouldHideHostname()` [C-537] | the 10 `...FolderName` methods are renamed `...Folder` and `shouldHideHostname()` is added [C-545] | no `getStoreUri`; renamed folder and flag methods [C-547] |
| `ImapFolder` (subclassed, `K9MailTransport.java:696`) | package-private [C-571]; the app needs it public | package-private [C-534] | package-private at A2 [C-541], public at A3 [C-543] | public [C-575]; no `ImapSearcher` hook |
| `search(ImapSearcher, listener)` and `executeSimpleCommand` (the fork's hook) | present, private [C-522, C-523] | **absent**; the only search takes a query string and flag sets [C-535, C-536] | absent [C-580, C-581] | absent [C-582] |
| `Folder.FolderType.HOLDS_MESSAGES` and `create(FolderType)` (`K9MailTransport.java:584`) | unchanged | unchanged | unchanged | `FolderType` has no `HOLDS_MESSAGES`; `create()` takes no argument; the abstract `Folder` class still exists [C-549, C-563, C-564] |
| `FetchProfile.Item.DATE` (`BackupImapStore.java:181`) | no `DATE` constant [C-527] | same | same | same [C-573] |
| `appendMessages`, `fetch(List, FetchProfile, listener)`, `TrustedSocketFactory.createSocket`, `BinaryTempFileBody`, `MimeMessageHelper.setBody`, `TextBody`, `MimeMultipart` | declarations identical to the vendored copy at A0 to A4 in the line comparison | | | |
| `Address(String, String, boolean)` (`PersonRecord.java:48`) | private [C-561] | private [C-539] | private | private [C-574] |
| `ImapMessage`, `ImapResponse`, `ImapSearcher` visibility (fork patch) | all three package-private [C-565, C-566, C-567] | same | `ImapMessage` public from A3 | `ImapMessage` public; the other two package-private [C-568, C-569, C-570] |
| `Message.getSize()` | returns `long` (vendored: `int`); no `getId` [C-560] | `long` [C-538] | same | `long`; no `getId` [C-562] |

The app does not call `Message.getSize()` or `getId()` on a k9 `Message` (`grep` in `app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail` finds only `PersonRecord.getId`), so that difference costs nothing here. The ones that do: the constructor (all candidates), the three-argument `Address` constructor and the visibility of `ImapResponse` and `ImapSearcher` (all candidates, one-word edits), the removed search hook (A1 onwards), and `StoreConfig` (A1 onwards).

*(b) The eleven fork-only commits (Q1.e) at each candidate.* The five that matter, with the cost of re-applying them. "Hunks overlapped" is a context test, not an application: the hunks of the fork patch (old-side line range, taken against the fork's parent) are compared with the hunks of `git diff -U0 -M <fork parent> <candidate>` for the same file, within two lines; a patch whose hunks are not overlapped is likely to re-apply, one whose hunks are overlapped needs a hand merge.

| Fork behaviour (commit) | A0 | A1, A2 | A3, A4 |
|-------------------------|----|--------|--------|
| APPEND with internal date ([C-034]; 4 hunks) | absent, the command has no date [C-524]; hunks overlapped 0 of 4 | absent; 2 of 4 | absent [C-554]; 2 of 4 |
| UID SEARCH on an arbitrary header (the hook and the visibility change, [C-036]; 4 hunks in `ImapFolder`, plus `Address`, `ImapMessage`, `ImapResponse`, `ImapSearcher`) | the hook exists, private: the visibility patch re-applies; 1 of 4 overlapped | **the hook is gone**: the method and its plumbing would have to be written again (new code; the query builder has no HEADER term [C-536]) | same as A1; `ImapFolder` public from A3 |
| `FetchProfile.Item.DATE` ([C-035]; 1 hunk in `FetchProfile`, 2 in `ImapFolder`) | absent [C-527]; 0 of 1 and 0 of 2 | absent; 0 of 1 and 1 of 2 | absent; 0 of 1 and 1 of 2 |
| read timeout 120 s ([C-448]; one line) | `SOCKET_READ_TIMEOUT = 60000` [C-525]; 0 of 1 | 60000; 0 of 1 (A1), 1 of 1 (A2: the line moved) | 60000 [C-553]; 1 of 1 (the line moved) |
| connect retry ([C-449]; 11 hunks) | one attempt per address [C-526]; 5 of 11 overlapped | one attempt per address; 7 of 11 | 7 of 11 |

The other six: *Add XOAuth2* ([C-033]): upstream has its own token-provider path from 2016-10-24 [C-455], so the fork's status-carrying `XOAuth2AuthenticationFailedException` (vendored `XOAuth2AuthenticationFailedException.java:7`) has no counterpart at any candidate and would have to be re-added to upstream's authentication code (a sixth patch) or P-2 arises (the app catches the type at `K9MailTransport.java:159,176,207,233,261,389`). *Fail early if username/password not set* ([C-450]; vendored `ImapStore.java:97,101`): the constructor of `ImapStore.java` at A0, A1, A3 and A4 contains neither "username not set" nor "password not set" (grep; A2 not grepped) [C-521, C-544, C-546]: a 9-line patch to re-apply. *Fix ArrayIndexOutOfBounds in the store-URI decoder* ([C-451]): already present at A0 to A3 [C-528]; the decoder is outside the module from A4 [C-460]. *SDK 7 compatibility* ([C-452]) and the two *publishing* commits ([C-453, C-454]): not applicable, as in Q9.a (c).

| `55370179` (U-044) | `ImapStore.closePooledConnections()`, 21 lines (`ImapStore.java:362`) | no: the method is declared only in the vendored copy at A0, A1, A3 and A4 (line comparison) | 1 file, 21 lines |
| `4616c097`, `e5445d56` (U-045, U-047) | SNI through `SSLParameters`, then the IP-literal and blank guard in `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory`, 66 insertions and 5 deletions | no: the upstream guard arrived on 2024-01-06 [C-487], after every candidate; no `SNIHostName` in the factory at A0 or A4 (grep) | 1 file, 71 changed lines |
| `bdea6f4f` (U-058) | mime4j 0.8.11 and commons-io 2.16.1 (the candidates declare 0.8.1 and 2.4 [C-532, C-552]), source adaptation to mime4j 0.8.x in 5 files, `org.apache.http.legacy` removed (`TrustManagerFactory`, 74 lines), webdav excluded | the candidates already target mime4j 0.8.1; `TrustManagerFactory` still uses the Apache `StrictHostnameVerifier` at A0 and A4 [C-531, C-548]; webdav is still inside the library at A0 to A2 (14 files) and outside it at A3 and A4 (tree listing) | the versions stay in this repository's `build.gradle`; whether the candidate sources compile against 0.8.11 is **not known without compiling**; `TrustManagerFactory` (1 file); webdav exclusion for A0 to A2 (build file and 2 files) |

Distinct vendored source files that the five commits changed: 10 (`ImapStore`, `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory`, `Address`, `Transport`, `EncoderUtil`, `MimeMessage`, `MimeUtility`, `MessageHeaderParser`, `TrustManagerFactory`, `RemoteStore`), plus `build.gradle`. None of this repository's changes is contained in a candidate.

*(d) Size of the change.* Upstream difference, merge base to candidate, main source only (tests, webdav and `mail/testing` excluded; the module move counts as renames with edits): `git diff -M --shortstat`: A0 73 files, +2,353 / -1,366; A1 79 files, +3,055 / -1,736; A2 96 files, +4,824 / -2,911; A3 155 files, +5,330 / -3,606; A4 163 files, +5,930 / -4,449. Effect on `k9mail-vendored/` (method: the path under `src/main/java` without extension, compared with the 102 compiled files; a `.java` to `.kt` conversion counts as the same file): files present in both / vendored only / candidate only: A0 100 / 2 / 16; A1 99 / 3 / 20; A2 98 / 4 / 32; A3 97 / 5 / 37; A4 95 / 7 / 42. Production files in the app: constructor calls in `BackupImapStore.java` and `K9MailTransport.java` (2), a new class that implements `OAuth2TokenProvider` to hand the stored token to the library (1, new), `BackupStoreConfig.java` (A1 onwards: 1 method added at A1; 10 renamed and 1 added at A2, A3; the `getStoreUri` path and 14 methods at A4); A4 adds a home for the store-URI decoder (the vendored `ImapStoreUriDecoder` is 133 lines) and the `FolderType` and `create()` edits in `K9MailTransport.java`. So, production files touched: **A0 2 edited + 1 new; A1, A2, A3 3 edited + 1 new; A4 3 edited + 2 new** (estimate by reading, not compiled); test classes that reference the store config, the store subclasses or the XOAUTH2 exception: 5 (a `grep` of `app/src/test`).

*(e) What is gained* is the next part.

**4. What the vendored copy is missing: a commit-message search, then ten fixes read**

*This is a commit-message search plus ten read commits. It is not a vulnerability audit, and a fix that is absent from the list proves nothing.* Method: non-merge commits between the merge base and the pin that touch the main-source paths (`k9mail-library/src/main`, `mail/common/src/main`, `mail/protocols/imap/src/main`) or the later homes of `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory` and `KeyChainKeyManager` (`app/core/...`, `legacy/core/...`), 531 commits; message (subject and body) matched case-insensitively with `git log --no-merges -i --grep=<keyword> --format=%H <merge-base>..<pin> -- <those paths>`, one run per keyword. Totals: security 3, vulnerab 0, CVE 0, STARTTLS 0, inject 0, certificate 9, hostname 8, verify 1, TLS 1, SSL 0, PREAUTH 0, downgrade 0, overflow 0, OOM 0, denial 0, crash 2, literal 1, sanitiz 0, escape 1, spoof 0, plaintext 0, cleartext 0, leak 1; **26 distinct commits**. Because the project's messages rarely use these words, I added a second, clearly separate pass with my own robustness keywords (NPE, NullPointer, endless, timeout, memory, invalid, unexpected, wrong, hang(s), infinite): NPE 2, NullPointer 1, endless 1, timeout 3, memory 1, invalid 3, unexpected 1, wrong 1, hang 0, infinite 0, **13 commits, 12 of them new**. The union is **38 commits, all listed below (0 more matched)**. By subject, 18 of the 38 are fixes or security-relevant changes (marked F, my reading); 20 are features, refactors, conversions or logging. The column "first candidate" is the first of A0 to A4 that contains the commit (`git merge-base --is-ancestor`).

*The ten read.* I chose them for an app that only talks IMAP over TLS to one configured server and builds and parses its own messages, and read each commit (message and diff). "Vendored copy has the flaw" is a reading of the vendored file, not a test.

| 2 | `171c0eca43` 2022-05-02 [C-486] | hostname check through httpclient5's `DefaultHostnameVerifier` instead of the legacy `StrictHostnameVerifier` | no: U-058 replaced the legacy verifier with the platform verifier and a session shim (`TrustManagerFactory.java:135-146`); whether the two verifiers differ on a given certificate was not measured (Q7.a) | 1 file, +4 -2 (+3 in the build file) | not needed |
| 3 | `9d02baad2c` 2024-01-06 [C-487] | no SNI for IP-literal hosts | no: the vendored factory has the guard (`DefaultTrustedSocketFactory.java:216`, used at `:239`; this repository's `e5445d56`) | 2 files, +10 -2 | not needed |
| 4 | `ce0d6f1d5c` 2026-10-26 [C-423] | certificate blocklist (SPKI SHA-256 of a compromised issuer) checked before system validation, in `TrustManagerFactory`; OkHttp added to `:core:common` | yes: no blocklist in `TrustManagerFactory.java` (0 matches for "blocklist" or "blocked") | 3 main files (2 new), +48 -5; plus a build-file dependency on OkHttp and okio for the hashing | new code: the vendored copy has no equivalent; the hash needs OkHttp's `CertificatePinner` or an own SHA-256 over the SPKI |

| 6 | `025502ceb5` 2017-01-12 [C-489] | an unknown content transfer encoding no longer throws; the data is passed through | yes, in another form: `MimeUtility.java:1053` throws the unchecked `UnsupportedOperationException` for an unknown encoding | 2 main files, +5 -10 | applies (the deleted exception class does not exist in the vendored copy) |

Result of the ten: the vendored copy has the flaw for 7 (items 1, 4, 5, 6, 8, 9, and 10 in code form only, off the app's path), does not have it for 2 (items 2 and 3), and I could not tell for 1 (item 7). Of the ten, the candidates contain: A0 2 (1, 6), A1 3 (adds 9), A2 3, A3 3, A4 4 (adds 8); C3 at `be7849eb5d` 4; C4 7; the pin all ten.

*Third-party libraries.* Vendored: mime4j 0.8.11, commons-io 2.16.1, jzlib 1.0.7, jutf7 1.0.0 (`k9mail-vendored/build.gradle:100,102,107,109`). The versions the analysis already cites for the pin are mime4j 0.8.15 and commons-io 2.22.0, with jzlib and jutf7 unchanged (Section 2, Q2.d). Whether a published advisory applies to the vendored versions: **not checked in this analysis**. The only commit in the history range whose message cites a CVE or advisory identifier is an OkHttp patch [C-484]; OkHttp is not one of the vendored libraries.

**5. Route R-B sizing**

- *Candidates for backport:* of the ten read, **8** can be ported (1, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10), of which 7 touch code that still exists in recognisable form in the vendored copy (the touched functions were compared: items 1, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10) and 1 needs new code (4, the blocklist); 2 are not needed because the vendored copy already has an equivalent (2, 3). Of the 18 fix-like commits in the list of 38, the other 8 were not read.
- *Size (estimate, method stated):* the sum of the added and deleted lines of the main-source hunks of the eight commits is 47 + 53 + 2 + 15 + 5 + 32 + 4 + 30 = **188 lines in 12 upstream main-source files**; in the vendored copy that is **5 edited files** (`DefaultTrustedSocketFactory`, `TrustManagerFactory`, `ImapResponseParser`, `MimeUtility`, `ImapConnection`) and **2 new files** (the blocklist and its exception), written in Java; upstream tests are not counted. App files touched: 0. Bumping mime4j to 0.8.15 and commons-io to 2.22.0 would add 3 components to `gradle/verification-metadata.xml` (none of the three is there today, Q8.e): at least 6 entries (a jar and a POM each), exact count not derived.
- *What a backport cannot bring:* anything that depends on later structural change: the token-provider seam, OAUTHBEARER, the `ENVELOPE` superset (Section 6, Section 5); fixes in classes that exist only as Kotlin `internal` types (they can only be re-expressed, as items 5 to 10 here are); fixes outside the mail modules (item 4's hashing lives in `:core:common`).
- *Who tracks future fixes, and what detects them:* the product owner, as for Option 3. Mechanism (not built): record in the decision the upstream commit up to which the paths were reviewed (the pin is not that commit: only 38 commit messages were searched and 10 read), and periodically run the same `git log --no-merges -i --grep=<the 23 keywords plus the 10 robustness words> <recorded commit>..<upstream head> -- <the paths above>` and read the result. Upstream's cadence in these paths is in Q9.d (6 commits in `mail/common` and 2 in `mail/protocols/imap` over the last three months).

**6. The routes compared**

Counted, not described. R-A candidates ruled out: **A2** (26 mail-path commits and 5 Kotlin files more than A1 for no further fix among the ten), **A3** (adds the two-module layout and 5 more files than A2 for no further fix among the ten), **C3** (`ImapFolder` is a final Kotlin class from 2020-06-08 and `StoreConfig`, `Folder` and `ImapSearcher` are gone: 27 of 32 names, and the app's subclassing design cannot be kept, so it needs Part B's redesign without Part B's benefits) and **C4** (25 of 32 names, internal classes, the `ImapStore.create` factory, JVM modules with Moshi and httpclient5; it is the pin one month earlier, so it is Option 1 with P-2, P-4 and P-5 intact and 7 of the 10 fixes).

| | Stay as is (Option 3) | Stay + backport (R-B) | R-A at 5.304 (A0) | R-A at 5.501 (A1) | R-A at 5.700 (A4) | Vendor the pin (Option 1) | Fork (Option 2) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| How current | fork base 2016-10-20; 732 mail-path commits behind | the same base | 2017-11-10; 625 behind | 2018-02-25; 599 behind | 2019-11-23; 493 behind | 2026-11-12; 0 behind | 0 behind |

P-1 (package strings), P-3 (`MessagingException`), P-4 (the factory slot) and P-5 (the `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory` producer) do not arise at A0 to A4 because the package names stay `com.fsck.k9.*` [C-521], `MessagingException` is not yet moved [C-475], the factory is still passed to the super-constructor [C-546] and `DefaultTrustedSocketFactory` is still in the library until 2022-04-28 [C-472]; P-2 arises because no candidate has the status-carrying type [C-576, C-577, C-578].

**Viable routes.** Stay (Option 3) and R-B are viable at no change to the app. R-A is viable only at A0, A1 and A4 and only as a fork-and-patch job: A0 changes least and keeps the fork's search hook; A1 is the tagged all-Java point (C1); A4 is the newest point at which the app's subclassing design still exists, with the largest app-side edit. Options 1 and 2 are as costed in Q9.a.

**The direct answer.** "Keep it vendored but use the latest version" is Option 1: the latest is the migration. Keeping it vendored on a newer snapshot short of the pin is possible at only a few points, between 2017-11 and 2019-11, and each is still a fork-and-patch job (the five fork behaviours, ten of this repository's patched files, and an OAuth token-provider adapter in the app) that reaches 2 to 4 of the ten fixes I read and leaves the module 493 to 625 mail-path commits behind; from 2020-06-08 the app's subclassing design cannot exist. Keeping today's code and backporting is the cheaper way to those fixes (8 of the 10, about 190 changed lines in 5 files plus 2 new, estimate) and the only vendored route that reaches the 2022 to 2026 fixes, but the module stays the 2016 code with chosen copies, found by a search that proves nothing about the rest. **Not known without building or applying anything:** whether any snapshot or backport compiles with the app and passes its tests; how many of the other 700 or so commits in these paths matter (only 38 messages were searched and 10 commits read); and whether an advisory applies to mime4j 0.8.11 or commons-io 2.16.1. The experiment that would settle the first: copy a candidate (or the vendored module with the eight diffs applied) into a scratch module outside the repository, apply the five fork patches and the ten-file changes there, compile the adapter against it and run the adapter's unit tests, as experiment (d) did for the pin. Not done here (Part A builds nothing).

## 10. Separability

Scope. The question is whether each of the two later product goals, RCS support and Google OAuth sign-in, can be done independently of replacing `:k9mail-vendored`, or depends on it. Evidence only: the analysis states a verdict per goal and does not decide what the product owner does with it (EPIC-MODERNIZATION-006 Out of Scope; `EPIC-MODERNIZATION-006:65-67`). Statements about this repository come from the commands shown (run 2026-11-13 at the working tree of the start commit); statements about upstream are at the pin.

| Goal | Separable / inseparable | Evidence (cited) |
|------|--------------------------|------------------|
| RCS support | **separable** | Nothing in the mail libraries, upstream or vendored, nor in this app's mail path depends on RCS; what RCS support would need is a question of what the app can read from the device, which the migration neither adds nor removes. Details in Q10.a. |
| Google OAuth sign-in | **separable** | Token acquisition and refresh are app code that never touches the mail library; the library receives only a token (today a pre-built string, after a migration through a provider interface whose implementation the app supplies). A later change of sign-in replaces the acquisition code behind that seam in either world. Details in Q10.b. |

### Q10.a RCS support

1. **The mail libraries have no RCS content.** At the pin: `git grep -niwE 'rcs|rich communication' <pin> -- mail core/common legacy/logging feature/account/api feature/mail/folder/api core/architecture/api feature/mail/account/api` returns **0 lines**; over the whole repository it matches **2 files, both binary** (a PNG and a web font), no text line [C-429]. In this repository: `grep -rniwE 'rcs|rich communication' k9mail-vendored` returns **0 lines**.
2. **The app's mail path has no RCS content.** `grep -rniw rcs app/src k9mail-vendored/src` returns **1 line**, a comment in `app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/mail/MmsSupport.java:138` ("Strip recipient if it's also the sender. Ensures that incoming messages in RCS threads between 2 people don't include your own phone number ..."), in code that reads the MMS provider; the comment is the app author's observation and is **not verified here** against a primary source. `grep -rn 'content://im' app/src k9mail-vendored/src` returns **0 lines**.
3. **What the app reads from the device is decided before the mail library is involved.** The backup queries are built in `app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/service/BackupQueryBuilder.java:79-93` and `:104-140` against three providers only: `Consts.SMS_PROVIDER` (`content://sms`), `Consts.MMS_PROVIDER` (`content://mms`) and `Consts.CALLLOG_PROVIDER` (`Consts.java:29,33,34`). The mail classes receive cursor data and build MIME from it (Section 2 rows 1, 4; Section 5 row 7 and the header table, where every `X-smssync-*` header is an app string). A further data source would be a new reader in the app and a new `X-smssync-datatype` value; neither the vendored library nor the pinned upstream API has a per-source coupling (the MIME build and parse calls of Section 5 take text, addresses and attachment bodies).
4. **What RCS needs from Android is not verified in this analysis.** Whether Android exposes RCS messages to a third-party app, and through which provider or API, was not established: no primary source for Android's RCS or messaging providers was opened in this analysis (the sources it used are the upstream repositories, the registries and this repository). Any statement about that is therefore **not verified in this analysis** and is the real open dependency of the RCS goal; it exists with or without the migration.
5. Consequence. The migration does not change that dependency, and RCS support does not wait for, or force, the migration: **separable**. Nothing is recorded for the product owner's decision under the epic's Out of Scope clause for this goal.

### Q10.b Google OAuth sign-in

1. **Acquisition is app code, outside the mail library.** `app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/auth/OAuth2Client.java` runs the web flow (`AUTH_URL` and `TOKEN_URL` at `:30-31`, scopes at `:105-108`, including the Gmail scope `https://mail.google.com/`); `auth/TokenRefresher.java:42` (`refreshOAuth2Token`) refreshes with the stored refresh token or through the `AccountManager`; `grep -rn "com\.fsck\.k9" app/src/main/java/com/zegoggles/smssync/auth` returns **0 lines**.
2. **The only crossing into the mail library today** is `AuthPreferences.getStoreUri()` (`preferences/AuthPreferences.java:247-262`), which writes `AuthType.XOAUTH2` and a pre-built SASL string from `generateXOAuth2Token()` (`:335-344`) into the store URI, and the failure path in which the worker refreshes the token when `XOAuth2FailedException.getStatus()` is 400 (`service/BackupWorker.kt:442-446`) (Section 6, Q6.d). The library only sends that string.
3. **After a migration** the library asks an `OAuth2TokenProvider` for a token before each attempt and calls `invalidateToken()` after a negative response [C-253, C-300]; the interface is in `:mail:common`, its only upstream implementation is in the Android module `:legacy:common` on AppAuth and is not consumable [C-333, C-334] (Section 6, Q6.c). So the app keeps its own acquisition either way, and the library sees only a token. The crossing changes (string to provider; the status gap is P-2, Section 5), the acquisition does not.
4. **Consequence for the later goal.** A move to a different Google sign-in replaces the acquisition and refresh code in `auth/` (and what `AuthPreferences` stores) in both worlds; it does not depend on which mail library is under it. What Google currently requires or offers for sign-in is **not verified in this analysis** (no Google primary source was opened) and is not needed for this verdict.
5. **Sequencing observation, not a dependency.** The provider seam exists only after the migration; before it, any new sign-in must still end in a pre-built XOAUTH2 string in the store URI. Doing the sign-in change first costs a string-shaped hand-over that the migration would then replace (Section 11 row 8); doing it after costs nothing extra. This is an ordering remark for the product owner; it does not make the goal inseparable.

### Q10.c Result

Both goals are **separable**. Predicate S (Section 12) is false. No inseparable dependency is recorded for the product owner under EPIC-MODERNIZATION-006 Out of Scope.
## 11. Sizing and split

Scope. This section sizes REQ-MODERNIZATION-019 Part B (the migration) from counts taken in this repository at the start commit and from Sections 2 to 9. It does **not** design Part B. The breakdown is **prose rows only**: no story, design, requirement, contract or change-record file is created, and no acceptance criterion is written (IC-4). Every row assumes CNTR-MODERNIZATION-007 and CNTR-MODERNIZATION-001 unchanged; a row that would need a contract change is labelled "conditional on CR (P-n)" naming the P item of Section 12 (P-1 to P-5), and contains no interface design. Vocabulary: `MailTransport` is the port, `K9MailTransport` its adapter, and the pair is the anti-corruption layer DES-MODERNIZATION-009 names. Migration styles used in the rows: **branch by abstraction** (introduce or use a seam, build the second implementation behind it, switch); **parallel run** (run old and new on the same input and compare); **one-step swap** (replace in a single change). If the recommendation of Section 12 is `no-go`, the breakdown below is **not to be executed**; the sizing then states the cost that the decision avoids.
